# Original rules ledger

What the retail game is documented to do, what the shipped data proves,
and what we still do not know. This is the F00-B.2 counterpart to the
content inventory: the inventory counts *what files exist*, this ledger
records *what the original rules are* and how well each is evidenced.

Every fact carries one classification:

- **verified_original** — confirmed against authored data in the local
  retail install or by an existing verified path in this tree.
- **documented** — stated in documentation shipped with the game, but
  not (yet) confirmed against data or observed behavior.
- **inferred** — reasoned from filenames, data patterns or prior
  research; not stated anywhere authoritative.
- **designed** — a deliberate rust-mm2 policy, not an original claim.
- **unknown** — requires research; provisional behavior does not count
  as an answer.

Rules imported from MM1, MM3 or MM2Hook are never assumed; if a fact
comes from community knowledge it says so explicitly and stays at most
*inferred* until verified locally.

## Sources and method

| Source | What it is | How it was read |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MM2HELP.HLP` | In-game help, 183 topics (cited as `help:<topic>`) | Decompiled locally with helpdeco (GPLv3, tool only — output not committed) |
| `Readme.rtf` | Retail readme, troubleshooting + MP notes (cited `readme:§`) | `textutil` |
| `Booklet.pdf` | Jewelcase booklet, controls/maps (cited `booklet:p`) | `pypdf` text extraction |
| `TROUBLE.RTF` | Crash-recovery troubleshooting doc | `textutil` |
| `data:<path>` | Authored files in the retail install | `mm2-inspect dump` / `inventory` through the read-only VFS |
| code docs | `docs/vehicle-handling.md`, `docs/research/*` | Prior verified/inferred work in this tree |
| `Midtown2.exe` | Retail executable (cited by address, image base `0x400000`) | `llvm-objdump`; full decompile with the Kuna decompiler v1.755 (2026-10-08, `docs/research/vehicle-physics/`) |

Retail install: `/Users/linus/coding/rust-mm2/retail`, enumerated
2026-09-20 (catalog fingerprint `fnv1a64:e91e6cd4b2ae30d9` at commit
`156b0cc`). Numbers below are reproducible with
`mm2-inspect inventory <install>` and `mm2-inspect dump <install> <path>`.

## Drivers, profiles and progression

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DRV-1 | A driver profile accumulates race history; multiple drivers can share one install. | documented | help:Creating or Loading a Driver; data:`players/` (17 files) |
| DRV-2 | Each driver has a fixed rank: Amateur or Professional. | documented | help:Creating or Loading a Driver |
| DRV-3 | Amateur = longer Blitz times, less traffic, easier unlocks. Professional = shorter Blitz times, more traffic, harder unlocks, earns Pro points. | verified_original | help:Creating a New Driver; data:`mm*data.csv` — the second parameter block per row consistently carries shorter TimeLimits and higher densities/opponent counts |
| DRV-4 | Pro points are awarded on two criteria: finishing position and vehicle driven (slower vehicle, more points). Exact formula unknown (UNK-8). | documented | help:Creating or Loading a Driver |
| DRV-5 | Best results are recorded per driver per race and viewable on Race Records / Driver's Stats, filterable by race type, city, and Amateur Times / Pro Times / Pro Points. | documented | help:Race Records, help:Driver's Stats Screen |
| DRV-6 | Records are kept only for races run under default conditions; customizing laps/checkpoints/opponents excludes that run. | documented | help:Race Records |
| DRV-7 | The last remaining driver cannot be deleted. | documented | help:Main Menu Screen |
| DRV-8 | Quick Race jumps straight into the last race played after vehicle select. | documented | help:Quick Race, help:Main Menu Screen |

## Race modes

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| RACE-2 | Each event has fixed authored location, checkpoint set, weather, time-of-day and densities. | verified_original | help:Blitz/Checkpoint/Circuit; data:`mm*data.csv` per-row Weather/TimeofDay/Ambient/Peds/Cops |
| RACE-3 | Finishing top-3 as Amateur or 1st as Professional in a Blitz/Checkpoint/Circuit race unlocks customization of that race's weather, time-of-day, traffic/ped/cop density (Circuit: laps + opponents, no pedestrians). | documented | help:Blitz Race, help:Circuit Race, help:Unlocking Races, help:Races Screen |
| RACE-4 | Cruise-mode weather/time-of-day/density options are always available, no unlock needed. | documented | help:Races Screen |
| RACE-5 | Destroying your vehicle in a Blitz or Checkpoint race means restarting from the beginning; in a Circuit race it costs a time penalty and the vehicle resets. | documented | help:Blitz Race, help:Checkpoint Race, help:Circuit Race, help:Durability |
| RACE-6 | The green compass arrow points to the nearest un-cleared checkpoint and turns yellow when that checkpoint is behind you; X/S cycle the arrow through remaining checkpoints. Artwork verified in the installation: `geometry/hudarrow{01,_blitz01,_cc01}.pkg` are flat-XZ chevron meshes (tip −Z, pivot at the local origin) each carrying two paint jobs — a family tile (`s_hudarrow_green` checkpoint/circuit, `s_hudarrow_red` blitz, `s_hudarrow_violet` crash course) plus the shared `s_hudarrow_yellow` — the authored ahead/behind colour pair the documented flip names. F22-A.5 binds them; the on-screen presentation is unrecovered (UNK-33, DSN-8). | documented | help:Using Dashboard Instruments, help:General Keyboard Game Controls; booklet:p6; `geometry/hudarrow*.pkg` + `texture/s_hudarrow_*.tex` on retail |
| RACE-7 | In Checkpoint races the finish line only appears after every checkpoint is cleared. | documented | help:Checkpoint Race, help:Tips for Winning |
| RACE-8 | Amateur rows: 4-7 opponents in Checkpoint/Circuit, 0 in Blitz; Professional rows: 4-7 (london checkpoint 6-7). | verified_original | data:`mmracedata.csv`, `mmcircuitdata.csv` |
| RACE-9 | Authored per-city selectable-event rosters: 12 Checkpoint, 10 Blitz, 10 Circuit rows in each city's `mm*data.csv`. | verified_original | `mm2-inspect inventory` event-metadata rows (both cities) |
| RACE-10 | More `.aimap` event files ship than the tables list (london race0-13/blitz0-12/circuit0-11, sf race0-11+r0/blitz0-13/circuit0-11 vs 12/10/10 rows). Whether the extra files are selectable events or dev leftovers is unknown (UNK-2). | verified_original | `mm2-inspect inventory` races family vs event-metadata rows |
| RACE-11 | `race/<city>/<stem>.aimap` binds the event's Amateur actor roster and `<stem>.aimap_p` the Professional one: `[Opponent]`/`[Police]` counts equal the corresponding `mmracedata.csv` block's Opponents/Cops on 23/24 checkpoint events. `sf/race0` is an authored anomaly — amateur Opponents=7 while `race0.aimap` wires 6 (`race0-a-6.opp` ships unreferenced). The `mm2-inspect opponents` audit (2026-09-21) re-measures the opponent half of this on *every* table kind: `sf/race0` amateur is still the only wired-vs-table count mismatch — every other event's `[Opponent]` rows match its authored count at both difficulties (Blitz wires 0 rows matching Opponents=0). | verified_original | measured cross-check `mmracedata.csv` vs all 24 checkpoint `race<N>.aimap{,_p}` (2026-09-20); `mm2-inspect opponents` all-table audit (2026-09-21); docs/research/aimap.md |
| RACE-12 | An `[Opponent]` row wires `geo <route>.opp <tail>`: the opponent's vehicle id (amateur rows field base cars — `vpbug`/`vpcoop`/`vpcab`/`vpauditt`…; professional rows shift to harder variants — `vpcoop2k`, `vpvwcup`, `vpdb7`, `vppanoz`, `vppanozgt`), an authored `.opp` driving line, and a numeric tail (10 values on race rows; decoded into the documented driving-parameter vocabulary — RACE-14/UNK-11). Many events ship spare `.opp` files no `[Opponent]` row references (43 london + 34 sf records, including `blitz3`/`blitz4` route files on 0-opponent events); non-table stems still carry lineups (`race/london/race12.aimap` wires 1; `race/sf/stunt0.aimap` wires 1 whose `opp-c0.2` is a dead ref). Every wired vehicle id resolves to a `ready` vehicle-catalog entry on retail. | verified_original | `mm2-inspect opponents` on retail (2026-09-21): 64 builds/city, 0 failed, 271 london + 246 sf opponents wired, 0 unresolved vehicle ids; docs/research/aimap.md |
| RACE-13 | `tune/vehicle/<id>_opp.vehcarsim` exists for nearly every stock car (23 files on retail) and is a *sparse override*, not a standalone tune: authored values differ from the base file (`vpbug_opp`: inertia box, drivetrain, end-train inertias, horsepower, top speed), but most `_opp` files omit fields the base file carries, and several (`vp4x4_opp`, `vpbus_opp`, `vpcab_opp`…) author transmission data in an alternate schema — `NumGears`/`GearRatios`/`UpshiftRPM`/`DownshiftRPM`/`DownshiftBias` — that the player files' `ManualNumGears`/`Low`/`High` band schema does not use. `vpsemi_opp` and `vpvwcup_opp` are complete under the base schema. The shipped executable never reads them: an opponent's car loads `tune/vehicle/<geo>.vehcarsim` through the player's own `vehCarSim` loader (`aiVehiclePhysics::Init` `0x5591a0` → `vehCar::Init` `0x42be00` → `vehCarSim::Init` `0x4cbb70`, geo name passed unchanged, directory fixed at `tune/vehicle`), and no `opp` token exists anywhere in `Midtown2.exe` (docs/research/opponent-ai.md). `mm2_content::load_opponent` matches it: an opponent loads the base `.vehcarsim` only, the `_opp` file unread (until 2026-10-04 it merged the `_opp` document over the base tune). | verified_original | `mm2-inspect list`/`dump` field audit of all 23 retail `_opp` files (2026-09-21) |
| RACE-14 | The ten-value `[Opponent]` tail is read by `sscanf("%s %s %f %d %f %f %d %d %d %d %d %f")` (`0x555154`) into a `0x58`-byte record preset with its defaults, and handed once to `aiVehiclePhysics::RegisterRoute` (`0x559660`) — nothing rewrites it during a race. Col 0 `maxThrottle` (default 1.0; retail 0.57–1.00) is the throttle the car drives at whenever it is not braking; col 1 `unkFlag` (0) is stored with no reader found; col 2 the obstacle look-ahead (default **50**; retail 50–150) — an obstacle farther along the planned path is ignored; col 3 `cornerBrakingThreshold` (default 0.7) — the car brakes for a bend only once its brake demand `v·(v−vc)/(23.76·d)` exceeds it, and then at that demand; cols 4–7 `avoidTraffic`/`avoidProps`/`avoidPlayers`/`avoidOpponents` (default 1) gate which obstacle classes the path sweep tests (ambient cars, props within 500 m, players, other opponents — `0x566ff0`); col 8 `weirdPathfinding` (0) changes which detour candidate is preferred (`0x55d980`); col 9 `cornerSpeedMultiplier` (default **1.0**; retail 0.89–2.29, professional rows reach 2.0+) multiplies every corner *speed*, `vc = sqrt(23.76·R)·mult`. The community headers' `RegisterRoute` defaults (75, 2.0) are not the parser's; every retail race row authors all ten values. Our opponents are driven by a learned policy (DSN-112), so only `maxThrottle` is consumed — as the ceiling on the driver's throttle, the authored difficulty dial within a field. The other nine columns parametrise the original's own controller (its corner speed, braking threshold, look-ahead and what its path sweep tests) and have no counterpart in a learned one: they stay decoded on `OpponentSpec` and unconsumed (retail authors `avoidPlayers=0` on 75% and `avoidOpponents=0` on 59% of rows). `unkFlag` has no reader in the executable at all (F15 review, #1362). | verified_original | `Midtown2.exe` `0x555154` (parser, defaults), `0x53d490`→`0x559660` (argument order), consumers `0x55b5f0`/`0x55b420`/`0x566ff0`/`0x55dda2`/`0x55d980`; retail `mm2-inspect dump` distributions on 536 rows (2026-09-24 re-measure); docs/research/opponent-ai.md, docs/research/aimap.md |

## Authored race records (waypoints, start grids)

|| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
|| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|| WPT-1 | `<stem>waypoints.csv` rows carry `x,y,z,a,w`; `w` supplies gate half-width across the street (the `Checkpoint.radius` field retains its historical name). Detection uses a finite vertical gate plane at the gantry centre, with swept movement required to cross it; approaching within that half-width no longer clears it. The plane test is designed in response to premature circle clears, not a verified retail collision rule. | inferred | data:`race/*/…waypoints.csv`; used by `mm2_content::race_def` |
|| WPT-2 | Row roles for Blitz/Checkpoint: row 0 is the start line, rows 1..n-1 are any-order checkpoints, the last row is the finish trigger. For Circuit every row after 0 is an ordered gate and the course closes through the start line again. | inferred | consistent across retail rows (e.g. blitz0 = line + 3 gates + finish, matching BLZ-1/CHK-1/CIR-1 docs); measured on all 24 checkpoint waypoint files 2026-09-20 — every file has ≥3 rows and a last row distinct from row 0 (london race0: 7 rows, finish ~840 m from the start; london race9's finish is closest at ~49 m); provisional |
|| WPT-3 | `<stem>_strtpnts` files hold the starting grid (one `x,y,z,a` row per slot). Only SF circuits ship them, under the short stem `cir<N>` — `cir1_strtpnts` … `cir9_strtpnts` — while the event rows are `circuit1`…`circuit9`; the same-index alias is the catalog's inference **and is wrong for most of them** (measured 2026-10-13 against each circuit's start line and `.opp` staging rows): `cir2`'s grid sits on circuit 0's start, `cir3`'s beside circuit 1's, `cir5`'s on circuit 8's, `cir8`'s by circuit 4's; only `cir1` (52 m from its start line) and `cir6` (53 m) agree with their index, the nearest mismatch being 475 m. The files look like a grid set for a different track order, and the shipped game reads none of them (UNK-17). A grid is therefore honoured only when its first slot lies within 200 m of the event's start line (`START_GRID_RANGE`); otherwise the designed single slot on the line stands (DSN-6). | inferred | data:`race/sf/cir*_strtpnts`; `mm2-inspect events` extras listing |
|| WPT-4 | The `a` columns are degrees in different conventions. `_strtpnts` `a` and `.opp` staging headings are vehicle yaw — forward `(−sin a, −cos a)`; `RaceStart.yaw_deg` converts directly to `Quat::from_rotation_y`. Waypoint `a` instead orients the gate normal `(−sin a, +cos a)` — gantry yaw is **negative** authored angle. Corrected 2026-10-03 against London circuit0: gate 1 `a=63.809998°` and closest `.opp` leg bearing `−58.305°` agree within 5.5° using the negative angle, versus 57.9° with the old sign (axes modulo 180°); the other oblique gates support the same sign. The previous general `atan2(dx,dz)`/180°-apart claim was incorrect for non-cardinal gates. `_strtpnts a=0` still means no heading (retail `cir6_strtpnts`); unset starts derive their course facing. Whether the original enforces gate direction remains open (UNK-16). | inferred | retail waypoint/`.opp` geometry, SF start grids; `Checkpoint::forward`, `mm2_app::checkpoint_gate`, `mm2_content::race_def` |
|| WPT-5 | All waypoint/start coordinates are in world space in the same frame as the PSDL city mesh. | verified_original | retail blitz0 waypoints coincide with the London street mesh the spawned car sits on (screenshot evidence) |

## Crash Course

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CC-1 | Two schools: London Cabbie (East End Cab Company) and SF Stunt Driver (Golden Gate Stunt Driving School). | documented | help:Crash Course, help:Crash Course Screens |
| CC-2 | Each school: 9 lessons, 3 midterms, 1 final — 13 authored events, ordered lesson1-3 → midtrm1 → lesson4-6 → midtrm2 → lesson7-9 → midtrm3 → final. | verified_original | help:Crash Course ("nine lessons", "three lessons in a group"); data:`mmcrashdata.csv` 13 rows in that exact order; `crash0-12` files |
| CC-3 | Passing each group of three lessons unlocks that group's midterm; passing all three midterms unlocks the final. | documented | help:Crash Course |
| CC-4 | Required vehicle per school: Ford Mustang Fastback (SF), London Cab (London). Passed lessons can be replayed in any vehicle. Implemented (F21-B.10, enhanced/designed reading): the menu launch of an unpassed lesson drives `vpbullet` (SF) / `vpcab` (London) — the catalog rows the help's names resolve to (`--list-cars`: "Ford Mustang Fastback"/"London Cab"), the same ids the CC-6 paint rewards bind — and a lesson with a beaten record replays in the pending selection. The required car does not rewrite the pending selection or the profile's remembered vehicle. Unverified: whether the original switches the car per lesson rather than per school, and whether a mid-course retry keeps it; `--event crash:N` on the CLI keeps the dev-chosen `--car`. | documented | help:Crash Course |
| CC-5 | "Work Experience" offers Blitz/Checkpoint races from the Crash Course screen. | documented | help:Crash Course |
| CC-6 | Rewards: SF midterms 1/2/3 → RSi Angel Cup paint / Double-Decker paint / Fastback paint, SF final → LTV; London midterms 1/2/3 → RSi Microsoft Cup paint / London Cab paint / Mini Cooper Classic paint, London final → Aston Martin DB7. | verified_original | help:Crash Course Rewards; data:`sf_rewards.csv`/`london_rewards.csv` — `crash,3/7/11` (the midterm row positions in the 13-row table) award `vpvwcup`/`vpddbus`/`vpbullet` paints (SF) or `vpvwcup`/`vpcab`/`vpcoop` paints (london), `crash,12` (the final row) awards `vp4x4`/`vpdb7` outright |
| CC-7 | Lesson composition: each `crash<N>` row owns a `crash<N>data.csv`/`crash<N>data_p.csv` sub-event table — every row names a waypoint CSV (`Filename`), an `Event` code, a `Checkpoints` count (1 on every retail row), a `TimeLimit` (0 = untimed on the two retail `corner` rows) and an `AmbDensity`, plus an unnamed/raw tail whose authored header names drift (`Misc`/`cornerspeed`/`chkflags`/`numopp`/`extra`/`etra`). `_p` = Professional is inferred from the shared `_p` convention and the tighter limits/higher density it authors (in one case a different waypoint file — sf `reverse180_p.csv`); the `Event` code selects one of eight per-frame handlers in the executable (0 jump, 2 follow, 3 cop-chase, 4 corner, 5 cross-traffic, 7 and 9 one shared maneuver/map handler, 8 stop; 1/6 have slots but are authored nowhere) — CC-8, CC-9. Own-stem `crash<N>.aimap{,_p}` wire the lesson's actors: follow lessons one `[Opponent]` lead car, cop-chase `[Police]` spawns + the single authored `[CopChaseDistance]` (sf crash5, 150), and the identical ten-road `[Exceptions]` block (roads 10–19) sf `crash1`/`crash2`/`crash4`/`crash12` each wire — london lessons author none. `<object>_crash<N>` records (`london_parkedcar_crash0.pathset`…) are the per-lesson world overrides — inferred suffix attribution. | inferred | data:`crash<N>data{,_p}.csv`, `crash<N>.aimap{,_p}`, `<object>_crash<N>.pathset` on retail (`docs/research/crashcourse.md`, `mm2-inspect crash-course`) |
| CC-8 | Crash Course sub-event loader and course (F21-C.2): `Midtown2.exe` `0x416080` reads `crash%ddata` (`…data_p` when the difficulty global `0x6b064c` is nonzero — the `_p` = Professional reading is now read, not only inferred) into ten 60-byte records of `Filename`, `Event` (int), `Checkpoints` (int, nonzero loads the course), `TimeLimit` (float s), `AmbDensity` (float, clamped to [0,1] and ×0.2 into `0x6b1e2c`, consumer untraced) and exactly three tail values: `tail[0]` the cornering minimum speed (float, mph), `tail[1]` a course flag (bit 0 of course `+0x20`, most likely gate-marker visibility — inferred from the neighbouring show/hide calls), `tail[2]` `numopp` (lead cars taken from the opponent array; the lead of sub-event *n* is the slot after the earlier sub-events' `numopp`). Later columns are never read. The waypoint rows become the course: row 0 is the start gate (counted passed), rows `1..` the gates; `Event 0` loads checkpoint mode 4 (gates in any order, the last row only after every other gate), every other event mode 5 (gates in authored order, the car's last segment tested against the current gate, complete when the passed counter equals the row count). Per-sub-event setup `0x415dc0`: the timer is on for events 0/3/5/6/7/8/9, on for 4 only when `TimeLimit > 0`, **off for 2 (follow ignores its `TimeLimit`)**; corner minimum speed = `tail[0]` if ≥ 1.0 else 50. Dispatch `0x4169a0` (table `0x416a2c`): 0→`0x4174d0`, 1→`0x419ca0`, 2→`0x417b20`, 3→`0x416a60`, 4→`0x4187b0`, 5→`0x418100`, 6→`0x417040`, 7 and 9→`0x419020`, 8→`0x419600`; above 9 logs an error. Every handler is the same seven-state machine on `+0x270` (0–2 title/Ready/Set, 3 running, 4 failed, 5 wreck recovery, 6 passed); a cleared non-final sub-event reloads the next record and replays the intro, the final clear posts the lesson passed (`0x41a700(1)`). Statically read from the disassembly; not run under a debugger. | verified_original | exe:`0x416080`, `0x416320`, `0x415dc0`, `0x4169a0`, `0x4346f0`, `0x4352a0`; `docs/research/crashcourse.md` "Recovered runtime" |
| CC-9 | Crash Course family rules (F21-C.2), state 3 of each handler. **Skeleton** (jump, cross-traffic, maneuver/map — nothing more): course complete → cleared, timer expired → "Time's up!", wrecked → "Game over!" (fails). **Follow** (untimed): cleared when the lead has finished its route or the course completes and the player is within 10 m of the lead ("You Won!"); failed when more than 100 m from it ("Car escaped!"); a wreck is recovered in place, not failed. **Cop chase**: at course complete, cleared unless a cop (the executable's cop array) with a clear ray is within 200 m ("You survived the gauntlet!" / "Lose your pursuers before you finish!"); timer expiry fails; a wreck is recovered. **Corner**: course complete clears (same cop test); timer expiry fails when timed; before the minimum speed is first exceeded, clearing a gate fails; once exceeded, more than 1 s below it fails (reset when above it again); a wreck fails; minimum = `tail[0]` mph (50 if unset), speed = `|forward speed|·2.236025`. **Stop** (timed): the lead's `MaxDamage` is set to 150000; lead wrecked → cleared ("You did it!"), lead finished its route → failed ("Car reached destination"), timer expiry fails, the player's wreck is recovered; the course flag is never read. Wreck recovery = 3 s then the car is reset in place (`0x406180`) and the lesson returns to running — implemented for follow, cop chase and stop (F21-C.4: `LessonWreck` + `resolve_lesson_wreck`, `WRECK_RESET_SECONDS`; the reset is the shared level-on-ground `ResetVehicle` landing plus a damage/rig/skin repair — what `0x406180` restores beyond the pose is not recovered); a wreck on the other families still restarts the whole lesson (designed, DMG-2), not the original's in-leg "Game over!" failure. Statically read; the "lead finished its route" test (`0x53d6c0`: last node, within 30 m of a goal point, past the goal plane inside 20 m) and the cop-sight ray polarity are partly inferred. | verified_original | exe:`0x4174d0`, `0x417b20`, `0x416a60`, `0x4187b0`, `0x418100`, `0x419020`, `0x419600`, `0x41a140`, `0x41a1c0`, `0x53d6c0`; `mmlang.dll` strings; `crates/mm2_game/src/lesson_rules.rs` + `tests/lesson_rules.rs` |

## Vehicle physics (original)

The complete reading is `docs/research/vehicle-physics/` (README first); these rows are its headline rules.

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PHY-1 | Cars, trailers, bangers and AI bodies fall at **19.6 m/s²**: each physics step adds `mass × −19.6` (read-only global `0x5c5c1c`) to the body force, and the wheels' static loads are built on the same constant, so the car model runs at 2 g. | verified_original | `Midtown2.exe` `0x46a110` (called from `0x42c680`, `0x440040`, `0x4d7af0`, `0x553650`), `0x4d23e0`; docs/research/vehicle-physics/01-rigid-body.md |
| PHY-2 | There is no fixed physics step: `Seconds` is the real frame delta clamped to [1e-4, 0.1] (the 1/60 cap is disabled by `RealTime(0)`), split into `min(3, ceil((dt − 0.001)·35))` equal samples — one step per frame above ~34 fps. Forces computed in a step are integrated at the start of the next. | verified_original | `0x4c6330`, `0x40170a`, `0x4688a0`, `0x412c72`/`0x412c87`; 01-rigid-body.md |
| PHY-3 | The body integrates momentum (symplectic Euler) with no damping; a wheel's suspension is integrated linearly-implicitly through the body (`0x478930`); clamps are 500 m/s and 4π rad/s per body axis for cars. `InitBoxMass` is the solid-box tensor `m(y²+z²)/12` etc. of `InertiaBox`. | verified_original | `0x477de0`, `0x478050`, `0x4760c0`, `0x4cbb70`; 01-rigid-body.md |
| PHY-4 | Tyres are per-axis stick–slip springs (`k = 2L/TireDispLimit`, damped at `TireDampCoef` of critical) whose displacement saturates at `μ(s)·N/k`, `μ(s)` a parabola in slip ratio peaking at `OptimumSlipPercent` (`StaticFric × surface friction`) and falling to `SlidingFric`; a friction circle couples the axes; forces act at the contact point. | verified_original | `0x4d34d0`, `0x4d25c0`, `0x4d24b0`; 02-wheel-suspension-tire.md |
| PHY-5 | Suspension: one ray per wheel; force `(cs·ẋ + ks·x)(1 + k2·x) + L` with `L` the static share of `m·19.6`, `ks = L/SuspensionExtent` at `SuspensionFactor 1` (Extent is the static sag), `cs = 2·sqrt(ks·L)·SuspensionDampCoef` (ζ ≈ 4.43×), `SuspensionLimit` a bump stop removing ¼ of the approach speed per step. | verified_original | `0x4d29f0`, `0x4d2710`, `0x4d23e0`; 02-wheel-suspension-tire.md |
| PHY-6 | Each drive train integrates one spin `ω += dt·τ/(I + dt·AngInertia)` (the file's drivetrain `AngInertia` is an implicit stiffness; `I = g²·I_engine + 0.02`, or `0.005·mass` for a free wheel), with a constant 50 N·m drag, per-wheel brake torque `BrakeCoef·StaticFric·r·L` (×1.2 when stopped), and a kinematic limited-slip bias ≤ 1.25 → 1.03; no traction control or ABS. | verified_original | `0x4d9e80`, `0x4d29f0`; 03-powertrain.md |
| PHY-7 | Engine torque follows a √5 polynomial with peak power exactly `MaxHorsePower` at `OptRPM` (1 hp = 746 W); the clutch opens below idle and closes above 2× idle (no stalling); `GCL` seconds of zero torque per shift; ratios put `OptRPM` at `Low`/`High` mph; automatic shift points bisect equal full-throttle power, biased by `UpshiftBias`/`DownshiftBias*`. | verified_original | `0x4d8e10`, `0x4d8f20`, `0x4cf210`, `0x4cf5f0`; 03-powertrain.md |
| PHY-8 | `vehAero` damps rotation per car axis with constant (`AngCDamp`), linear (`AngVelDamp`) and quadratic (`AngVel2Damp`) angular decelerations scaled by inertia, never reversing the spin in a step; drag is `Drag·|v_fwd|·v`, downforce `Down·v_fwd²`. | verified_original | `0x4d9350`; 04-chassis-and-assists.md |
| PHY-9 | `vehCarSim::Update` feeds the wheels: `SSSValue`/`SSSThreshold` speed-sensitive steering (disabled — threshold 0 — on every retail car), rear wheels counter-steer through their own `SteeringLimit`, Ackermann via `SteeringOffset`, the handbrake reaches only the rear wheels with the outside wheel relieved by `(1 − |steer|)`, and brake + throttle > 0.95 below 1 m/s releases the rear brakes (burnout). | verified_original | `0x4cc8d0`, `0x4cc880`, `0x4d3f70`; 04-chassis-and-assists.md |
| PHY-10 | `vehGyro` adds yaw torque `I_y·Drift·s|s|·ω_drive` every step and, while the handbrake is held, `I_y·Spin180/Reverse180·s·ω_drive` — both only with all four wheels grounded; `Pitch`/`Roll` level the car in the air while braking (0 in retail). `vehStuck` turns a wedged car in place at `Turn` rad/s and rights a car off its wheels. | verified_original | `0x4d5bf0`, `0x42c680`, `0x4d6130`; 04-chassis-and-assists.md |
| PHY-11 | Keyboard steering is a rate ramp toward ±1 with per-car `tune/<car>.asnode` rates and an output exponent interpolated by speed (`t = clamp(v, SpeedBaseLow, SpeedBaseHi)/(Hi − Lo)` — no `−Lo`); the full authored lock is available at any speed. Every human input is quantised to a byte (steer /127, pedals /255). | verified_original | `0x52dad0`, `0x405760`, `0x406320`, `0x4069e0`; 05-player-input.md |
| PHY-12 | Auto reverse (automatic box, option on): gear ≥ 1st, speed < 5 m/s, brake > 0.8, throttle < 0.1 → reverse with pedals swapped; back to first when the (swapped) pedal drops below 0.8. Below 4 mph with zero throttle the handbrake is forced on. Post-race: brake 1, steer full left. | verified_original | `0x414a10`, `0x405760`; 05-player-input.md (CTL-1, CTL-3) |
| PHY-13 | AI cars (opponents, police) drive the player's car model with the automatic box; an AI car touching the player that step gets `CarFrictionHandling 2`, halving its grip on any surface with friction < 1 (tarmac is 0.9); braking for a bend multiplies its angular momentum by 0.85 per frame; police at full throttle below 50 m/s multiply linear momentum by 1.03 per frame; race opponents have no rubber-banding. Ambient traffic is rail-driven and becomes one of 32 pooled simple bodies on contact. | verified_original | `0x55ad10`, `0x55b14e`, `0x469610`, `0x53dc50`, `0x553200`; 06-ai-input.md |
| PHY-14 | Engine constructor/init/reset set actual angular speed to zero while displayed RPM and RPM-at-shift begin at idle; the initial GCL torque cut is active. Idle torque brings the cold engine toward idle over subsequent steps. | verified_original | `0x4d8c00` → `0x4d8cd0`, store `0x4d8ce6`; Init `0x4d8d10` virtual reset `0x4d8d95`; 03-powertrain.md (2026-10-10) |

## Damage and recovery

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DMG-1 | A Damage Meter HUD bar runs green → yellow → red; at zero the vehicle stops working. | documented | help:Using Dashboard Instruments |
| DMG-2 | Destruction in Blitz/Checkpoint = restart race; in Circuit = time penalty + reset (RACE-5). Crash Course (CC-9, F21-C.4): a wreck on a follow, cop-chase or stop leg resets the car in place after 3 s and the leg goes on; on any other leg family the port restarts the lesson. The help-text reading of Blitz/Checkpoint is *not* implemented — operator recollection of retail play contradicts it (see DSN-68). | documented | help:Durability |
| DMG-3 | Vehicles have "more detailed damage modeling and breakaway parts". | documented | booklet:p3 |
| DMG-4 | In Cops & Robbers, damaged vehicles not carrying gold heal over time. Implemented (F05-AC04 slice): `cnr_host_step` heals every connected non-carrier on the authority each fixed step (`VehicleDamage::heal`); clients see it through damage replication. The rate is **designed**: no retail `RegenerateRate` is non-zero and the original's rate is unrecovered, so a full bound heals in `HEAL_SECONDS_TO_FULL` (30 s) unless a record authors a positive `RegenerateRate`, which wins. Disabled (wrecked) cars do not heal: the session's disabled-vehicle policy owns them (designed). | documented | help:Cops & Robbers |
| DMG-5 | `.vehcardamage` tune files exist per vehicle and decode to a typed model: `MaxDamage`/`MedDamage`/`ImpactThreshold`/`RegenerateRate`/`TextelDamageRadius`, two smoke pivots (`SmokeOffset`/`SmokeOffset2` + `DoublePivot`/`MirrorPivot`) and a flat embedded particle spec sharing the `dgBangerData` `BirthRule` vocabulary. 20 retail records, uniform 38-field set; `MirrorPivot` adds a 39th field on 7 (vpbullet, vpbus, vpcaddie, vpcop, vpddbus, vpdune, vpmustang99 — all authored 0). Whether/how the original consumes each field is unverified (UNK-13). | verified_original | data:`tune/vehicle/*.vehcardamage`; `mm2-inspect damage` + `dump` retail census 2026-09-22; docs/research/damage.md |
| DMG-6 | Authored damage bounds: `ImpactThreshold` 1500 on 19 of 20 records (100 on vpcaddie), `RegenerateRate` 0 on every record, `MedDamage < MaxDamage` on every record; `MaxDamage` spans 187.5k (vpcoop/vpcoop2k)–3.28M (vpsemi) tracking vehicle mass (impulse-scale, inferred); `TextelDamageRadius` spans 0.4 (vpcaddie/vpcentury)–20.0 (vp4x4/vppanozgt); `DoublePivot` is 1 on vpddbus/vppanoz/vppanozgt, 0 elsewhere. `vpmoonrover` ships no damage record — an authored absence, not an install gap. | verified_original | `mm2-inspect damage` + `dump` retail census 2026-09-22 |
| DMG-7 | `.vehstuck` records exist per vehicle: `Turn`/`Rotation`/`Translation`/`TimeThresh`/`PosThresh`/`MoveThresh` (20 files, uniform set) — the authored stuck-detection thresholds. Values: `Turn` ≈ π on 10 of 20 (vpford 3.098593), 1.57 on 6, others on vpbus/vpcentury/vpddbus/vpsemi; `Rotation` 0 on all; `Translation` ≈ 0.1 (vpcoop 0.164); `TimeThresh` 2.0 on 6, ~1.0 elsewhere; `PosThresh` 1.25 and `MoveThresh` 1.75 uniform — MoveThresh exceeds PosThresh on every record. MM2Hook's recovered `vehStuck` struct (2026-09-22) confirms the detector shape — `m_State`, `m_StuckTime`, `m_LastImpactPos`, squared thresh copies. Its `Update()` (`0x4d6130`) is now recovered (2026-10-08): wedged within `PosThresh` for `TimeThresh` with throttle > 0.75 and \|steer\| > 0.5 it yaws the body in place at `Turn·|steer|·steer` rad/s; with no wheel on the ground it rebuilds an upright matrix and lifts by `Translation` (the `Rotation` impulse path is dead — 0 on every record); moving beyond `MoveThresh` disarms. What arms it (an impact callback) is not traced. The implemented test combination is still designed (DSN-20). | verified_original | data:`tune/vehicle/*.vehstuck`; `mm2-inspect damage` + `dump` 2026-09-22; MM2Hook `src/modules/vehicle/stuck.h`; `Midtown2.exe` `0x4d6130`/`0x4d60f0`/`0x4d6500`, docs/research/vehicle-physics/04-chassis-and-assists.md |
| DMG-8 | `.vehgyro` records exist per vehicle: `Drift`/`Spin180`/`Reverse180` always, `Roll`/`Pitch` on 17 of 21 (absent on vpbug, vpcab, vpford, vpvwcup_angel; 0.0 where present) — the authored spin/drift/righting rates. MM2Hook's recovered `vehGyro` carries the same fields plus `Spinable`/`Driftable`/`Rightable` asNode gates. Its `Update()` (`0x4d5bf0`) is now recovered (2026-10-08): with all four wheels down, `Drift` adds yaw torque `I_y·Drift·s|s|·ω_drive` (s = steering input, ω_drive = the driven train's wheel spin) on every step, and while the handbrake is held `Spin180` (rolling forward) / `Reverse180` (rolling back or stopped) add `I_y·k·s·ω_drive`; `Pitch`/`Roll` level the car in the air only while braking. Imported cars now use this recovered gyro law; DSN-22 records the remaining contact/integration adaptations. | verified_original | data:`tune/vehicle/*.vehgyro`; `mm2-inspect damage` + `dump` 2026-09-22; MM2Hook `src/modules/vehicle/gyro.h`; `Midtown2.exe` `0x4d5bf0`/`0x42c680`, docs/research/vehicle-physics/04-chassis-and-assists.md |
| DMG-9 | Vehicle body shaders carry damage-state texture pairs: a PKG shader naming `<stem>_dmg` is the damaged variant of `<stem>`. MM2Hook's recovered `fxTexelDamage::Init` (a `vehCarModel` member fed by `vehCarDamage`'s `TextelDamageRadius`/`ImpactsTable`, DMG-5/UNK-13) strips the suffix at the last `_` case-insensitively and binds the clean texture — the `_dmg` original is stashed per shader slot and its texels are blitted onto a cloned live texture at impacts; a failed clean lookup keeps the `_dmg` name. Observed on retail: damage-region body sections are *authored* bound to `_dmg` shaders — `vpbug` BODY_H sections 4–6 (the car's whole right half, `x>0`) name `vpbugyellow_{sd,ft,bk}_dmg`; 22 of 27 base `vp*.pkg` (excluding `_dash`/`_trailer` variants) ship `_dmg`-bound BODY sections (vpbus 12 sections/6 slots; vpcop, vpdb731, vpmoonrover, vpmustang99, vpvw_dune none). `ApplyDamage`'s radius/barycentric mechanics and `Reset` are recovered and implemented (F05-B.9, `mm2_game::texel`/`mm2_app::texel_fx`); the splat shape (`ApplyBirdPoopDamage`) stays unrecovered — a designed radial blit (DSN-32). | verified_original | MM2Hook `src/modules/effects/texeldamage.{h,cpp}` + `src/modules/vehicle/carmodel.{h,cpp}`; `mm2-inspect dump` section/shader census of retail `geometry/vp*.pkg` 2026-09-23 |
| REC-1 | Player vehicles carry authored breakaway parts: `BREAK<NN>` chunks inside `geometry/<id>.pkg`, bound to `geometry/<id>_break<NN>.mtx` transforms and `tune/banger/<id>_break<NN>.dgbangerdata` fragment records (the banger `<base>_break<N>` convention, WLD-15). Naming reads as corner pieces (`BREAK0`–`BREAK3`) plus edge panels (`BREAK01/12/23/03`) — inferred. 14 catalog vehicles ship parts; vpsemi/vpftruck/vplafrance the largest sets (6 pieces each). Retail also ships 10 dead fragment records (vpeagle `break0/1` with no pkg at all, vpvw_cup/vpvwcup `break01/02`, all four vpvwcup_angel). Detachment rules — damage threshold vs impact — unverified (UNK-13). | verified_original | `mm2-inspect damage` + `mm2-inspect banger` retail census 2026-09-22; docs/research/damage.md |
| REC-2 | Vehicle breakaway detachment is **impact-driven, not damage-driven** (UNK-13 narrowed, read from `Midtown2.exe` 2026-10-11). `vehCarDamage`'s impact leaf (`0x4cb130`) runs only for an impact whose impulse (impact-record field `+0x34`) exceeds `ImpactThreshold`; it adds that impulse to the accumulated damage (`0x4caeb0`) and, in the same call, hands the *same impulse* and the contact point to the car model's break manager (`0x4d8890`). The manager's gate is one constant per manager — `10000.0` set by its constructor `0x4d8570`, never overwritten for the vehicle's manager — not the accumulated damage and not any part's `ImpulseLimit2`. A passing impact detaches exactly **one** part: the attached part whose anchor is nearest the contact point (squared distance scan), through `0x4d8930` (spawns a banger instance of the part's record, pose from the car matrix, random kick from the manager's speed ranges). The part list is the `break0..3`, `break01/12/23/03` geometry loaded by `vehCarModel::Init` (`0x4cd776`, slots `0x1e..0x25`). Nothing on this path reads the part record's `ImpulseLimit2` — that field only governs the *spawned fragment's* later collisions through the ordinary banger gate (`0x469610`, banger.md OPR-1.3). The port's DSN-21 now implements this gate — one nearest part on the delivered impulse — where the retired per-part `severity × part_mass > ImpulseLimit2` reading was the earlier designed departure. Separately **damage-driven**: `vehCarDamage::Update` (`0x4cadf0`) calls `0x4cdc90` on the car model once accumulated damage `>= MaxDamage`, which strips the second part manager's entries (model `+0xac`: `whl0..3`/`hub0..3`/`variant%d`; only `whl0..3` exist on any retail car, so a recovered "pair" is one wheel) in random corner pairs keyed on `SpeedMPH` — `vehCarSim +0x24c` = `Speed(+0x248) × 2.236025` (docs/research/vehicle-physics/04 § `vehCarSim` fields), thresholds `> 100` every wheel, `> 75` two random pairs plus a `variant` bit, `> 50` one pair, else nothing — each through the shared leaf `0x4d8930`, which refuses a part already detached (`part[+0] == 0`); the kick speed is `Speed × 1.3` (`0x5af3ec`), the episode is one-shot (`+0xa4`, marked on every path), and the model reset `0x4cdfe0` clears the flag and restores the full mask. Implemented as F05-B (`mm2_app::breakaway::eject_wheels`); the fragment kick's *direction* (random components scaled by the manager's `+0x14` ranges in `0x4d8930`) is the one part left unrecovered and is drawn as designed presentation. | verified_original (disassembly) / designed (kick direction) | `Midtown2.exe` `0x4cb130`, `0x4caeb0`, `0x4cadf0`, `0x4d8570`, `0x4d8890`, `0x4d8930`, `0x4cdc90`, `0x4cdfe0`, `0x4cd776`; docs/research/damage.md § Original breakaway rule |
| REC-3 | The impact-record impulse (`vehCarDamage` slot `+0x34`, read by the leaf `0x4cb130`) is a **solved contact impulse, kg·m/s (N·s)**, so `MaxDamage`, `ImpactThreshold`, the damage accumulator and the break manager's `10000.0` (REC-2) all live on one scale (measured 2026-10-12). The car's collision callback (`0x4caee0`, registered `0x4cb340`) stores `|J| × share` where `J = (1+e)·K⁻¹·(−v_rel)` is the contact solver's per-contact impulse vector (`0x46b7c0`; the same `K⁻¹(−v_rel)` as OPR-1.3, friction-clamped, divided by the manifold's contact count) and `share` is 1.0 against the world, `m_other/(m_other+m_self)` against a dynamic body. A slot per other body (12, keyed by body) re-offers the impact to the break manager only when it grows past 1.25× its last value; smaller above-threshold contacts still add to the damage accumulator. Our `ImpactEvent.severity × mass` (approach speed × mass, pre-solver) is the same unit but a lower bound for a wall hit (no `(1+e)`, full not effective mass) and an overestimate for car-on-car (`≈ (1+e)/4` of ours at equal masses), so DSN-21/DMG thresholds compare in kind, not in magnitude. Per-car retail masses/restitutions are not measured here. | verified_original (disassembly) / inferred (that `+0x10` on `K`'s diagonal is inverse mass; magnitude vs our estimate) | `Midtown2.exe` `0x4caee0`, `0x4cb340`, `0x4cb130`, `0x46b610`, `0x46b7c0`, `0x478cc0`, `0x4cb640`; docs/research/damage.md § Original breakaway rule |

| COP-15 | **No offense/heat counter in the cop's target selection** (#1300, 2026-10-14; disassembly of `Midtown2.exe`, `objdump -d -M intel` over `0x53dfb0`, the routine `aiPoliceOfficer::Update` (`0x53dc50`) calls first). It is stateless per frame: it walks the linked list of players at `0x6b1f60` (next at `+0x2c`), and for each takes the squared distance between the cop (`this+0xcc`+0x90) and that player's car against one global squared threshold (`0x5b4598`), then two further tests (`0x53e280` on the cop, then the line-of-sight query `0x550f80`); the first player passing all of them becomes the perpetrator (`this+0x9774`) and the cop's state word (`this+0x977a`) is set to 2. A second pass visits the ambient-car records (count word `0x6b1e5c`, one flag word per record at `this+0x9780`, already-flagged records skipped): the nearest-in-range record whose per-record float at `+0x30` is positive is flagged when a random draw (`0x4bbe20`) is below its float at `+0x34`, then the same sight query decides whether it becomes the perpetrator. Nothing in either pass reads or writes a per-player counter, accumulator or wanted level: the only per-record state is that boolean flag, and the choice is a function of distance, sight and one random draw. So 'several offenses on one tick' has no state to advance: it is a **documented non-goal**, not a test gap, and the F20 spec's 'multiple offenses one tick' edge case is satisfied by the existing sight-only model (`mm2_game::police::Pursuit` advances at most one phase per tick however many triggers coincide). **Not recovered:** what `0x53e280` tests, what the ambient-record floats at `+0x30`/`+0x34` mean (a speeding/violation probability is plausible but is a reading, not evidence), and the bust/arrest outcome (UNK-9 stays open; COP-9's 'no bust invented' stands). Mirroring the ambient branch would be inventing a rule, so it is not implemented. | verified_original (no counter in `0x53dfb0`; reading of the disassembly, not a runtime capture) / unknown (`0x53e280`, ambient `+0x30`/`+0x34` meaning, outcome) | disassembly: `objdump -d -M intel --start-address=0x53dfb0 --stop-address=0x53e280 $MM2_RETAIL/Midtown2.exe`; code check only (QUALITY-GATES level 1) |

## Multiplayer

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MP-1 | Up to 8 players total ("seven other people") via MSN Zone, IPX or TCP/IP; serial/modem are 1v1. | documented | help:Types of Multiplayer Connections, help:Multiplayer Screen |
| MP-2 | Max 10 concurrent hosted sessions per LAN. | documented | readme:§7.05 |
| MP-3 | Host controls connection type, password, max players, event, location, conditions, difficulty (from host's driver rank), and which vehicles/checkpoint races are unlocked — the host's record applies to everyone. | documented | help:Hosting vs. Joining |
| MP-4 | Modes: Cruise, Blitz, Checkpoint, Circuit, Cops & Robbers. No ambient traffic, cops or AI opponents in MP races; humans replace AI opponents. (The text says *races*; networked Cruise's traffic is DSN-71's enhanced policy, not a claim about the original.) | documented | help:Multiplayer Games |
| MP-5 | Race joiners must be in before the host starts; Cruise and C&R allow join/leave at any time. A leaver's vehicle disappears for everyone; host disconnect → a new host is designated. | documented | help:Multiplayer Games |
| MP-6 | No pausing in multiplayer (ESC still opens the menu). | documented | help:Pausing and Resuming, help:Displaying a Map |
| MP-7 | Host can eject players via F6 (in-game player list) or the lobby Eject button. | documented | help:Multiplayer Games, help:Multiplayer Lobby Screen |
| MP-8 | Lobby has chat, per-player vehicle/color, team pick (team modes), Ready (joiner) / Start Server (host), Game Settings box. | documented | help:Multiplayer Lobby Screen |
| MP-9 | MP-specific race data is thin: `copchase`/`multicop`/`*_p` records exist. (Superseded for C&R by CNR-6…CNR-10: `multicopwaypoints.csv` is the gold/hideout/bank site pool, and the markers, dots and cue tables ship too — `mm2-inspect cnr`.) The 99 `.aimap_p` variants once suspected as MP files are evidenced as Professional-difficulty actor rosters instead (RACE-11). | verified_original | `mm2-inspect inventory` multiplayer family; RACE-11 cross-check |

## Cops & Robbers

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CNR-1 | A gold bar spawns somewhere in the city; deliver it to your hideout to score, then another appears; carriers can be rammed to drop the gold, which anyone can pick up by driving over it. | documented | help:Cops & Robbers |
| CNR-2 | Three variants: Free-for-all (any unlocked vehicle, own hideout), Cops vs. Robbers (cops = Mustang Cruiser → bank; robbers = Mustang GT → hideout), Robbers vs. Robbers (teams, any unlocked vehicle, several hideouts). | documented | help:Cops & Robbers, help:Host Settings Screen |
| CNR-3 | Host options: location, weather, time-of-day, pedestrian density, match limit (none / time / points), gold mass (weightless / ¼ ton / ½ ton — heavier slows the carrier and holds the gold better). | documented | help:Cops & Robbers, help:Host Settings Screen |
| CNR-4 | Non-carriers heal over time (DMG-4). Results show individual points; team variants also show team totals. | documented | help:Cops & Robbers |
| CNR-5 | Drop physics, what knocks gold out of a carrier, respawn timing, cop/robber scoring beyond delivery, carrier-disconnect and out-of-bounds handling: not in shipped docs and not recovered from code or data. | unknown | UNK-10 |
| CNR-6 | Site pool: gold, hideout and bank positions come from `race/<city>/multicopwaypoints.csv` — 44 rows (sf), 46 (london), ordinary waypoint CSVs — drawn with `rand() % (rows − 1)` per site; an optional `multicopsets.csv` (triples bank/gold/hideout) is read first but ships on no retail install; under 3 rows the game falls back to arbitrary world positions. The distinct-sites re-draw is conditional on a flag whose setter is unknown, and whether `rows` counts the header is not established. | verified_original | docs/research/cnr.md §1; `mm2-inspect cnr` |
| CNR-7 | Host options are one packed integer: variant (0/1/2), gold mass index → **0 / 100 / 200** engine units, limit type (none/time/points), limit value → time **5/10/20/30 min** or points **100/250/500/1,000**; `mmlang.dll` labels agree. The kilogram meaning of 100/200 (help: ¼/½ ton) is not recovered. Variant numbering: 0 individual, 2 red/blue teams read from code; 1 = Cops vs. Robbers by elimination. | verified_original (values); inferred (variant 1, kg) | docs/research/cnr.md §2 |
| CNR-8 | A delivery scores **100** points, removes the gold's mass from the carrier, clears the carrier and has the host draw the next sites; a hideout/bank marker's radius field is 12.0. The pickup path awards 25 points (trigger — first pickup vs. steal — unknown). Gold is arbitrated by the host: a pickup request is honoured only with no current carrier. | verified_original (constants); inferred (radius test direction) | docs/research/cnr.md §3 |
| CNR-9 | Gold mass is added to the carrier's body on pickup and removed by the negated amount on every exit path read (deliver, drop, robbed); the local carrier's `carsim+0x40c` scalar is set to 1.0 / 0.9 / 0.81 for 0 / 100 / 200 units and reset to 1.0 (what force it scales is not identified). | verified_original (values); unknown (effect) | docs/research/cnr.md §3 |
| CNR-10 | The original announces get / drop / stash / recover per role (`ROB*`/`COP*` `GETLOOT`/`DROPLOOT`/`STASHLOOT`/`RECOVERLOOT`) and has/stashed/dropped per team (`BLUE`/`RED`); both city tables author all 14 families. Who may recover, and what it scores, is unknown. | verified_original (names); unknown (triggers) | `aud/spchdata/cnr{sf,london}.csv`; `mm2-inspect cnr` |
| CNR-11 | Gold rule core (F27-B.1): designed. `mm2_game::gold::GoldMatch` keeps one ownership state (resting / carried / dropped), arbitrates a tick's pickup requests (nearest host-measured car, ties to the lower player id, so arrival order does not matter), refuses stale rounds, applies the 100-point delivery once, ends on the point/time limit (individual in FFA, team total otherwise), and derives the carrier's mass/handling load from the state so it cannot leak. Policies where the original is unrecovered — pickup radius 5 m, 1 s dropper lockout, disconnect drops the gold in place, out-of-bounds gold re-placed at a fresh site with no score, ties reported as ties, 25 points on every grant, red→hideout/blue→bank draw — are enhanced policy or implementation choices, each labelled where defined. No wire/HUD/vehicle consumer yet. | designed | docs/research/cnr.md "authoritative gold state machine"; `mm2_game::gold` tests |
| CNR-12 | Gold host consumers (F27-B.2): designed. `mm2_app::cnr` drives `GoldMatch` from the authority's own car positions each fixed step (clock, vanished participant leaves, pickup contacts, delivery attempt, out-of-bounds re-placement below `WorldFloor`) and reconciles each car's `Mass`/`AngularInertia` against `load_for` from a recorded base, so the carrier's mass applies once, ends with the carrying and is stripped when the match resource is removed at session teardown (a client does the same from its `CnrReplica` with the load derived from the session's own settings). Enhanced policy where the original is unrecovered: a car-on-car impact at ≥ 8 m/s (`DEFAULT_DISLODGE_SEVERITY`) or a `Disabled` carrier drops the gold; wall/prop hits never do; a participant seen once and then gone has left. The load's handling scalar is recorded but deliberately *not* applied to the physics (what it scales is unidentified, UNK-10). F27-B.2b: `CnrHost::from_content` builds the match from the city's `CnrContent` site pool and the lobby settings' rules, and `spawn_cnr_markers`/`sync_cnr_markers` put the retail `wpobj_gold`/`pt_hideout`/`pt_bank` models at the drawn sites (visual only, no collider; a model that fails to load is counted, never substituted). Which marker a side delivers to follows `DeliveryTarget`; that the gold marker is hidden while carried is an implementation choice (what the original draws on a carrier is unrecovered). F27-B.3: the match is replicated host → client as protocol v21 `Message::Cnr` (state, not events; newest `(revision, elapsed)` wins; malformed or foreign frames refused counted) into a client-side `CnrReplica` — clients display it and cannot declare a pickup or score (a client frame is dropped `Malformed`); see `docs/research/net.md`. F27-B.4a: the host's choices are a session mode, `SessionMode::CopsAndRobbers(CnrSettings)` (variant, gold mass, limit — the option tables and rule constants now live in `mm2_game::cnr_options`, re-exported by `mm2_content::cnr`), carried in the lobby advertisement *by name* (not the executable's inferred variant numbering), validated (city world only; a limit of 0 or beyond 1440 min / 1,000,000 pts refused) and gated client-side on the city's site pool (`check_session`, ≥ 3 sites). Enhanced policy / implementation choice: a started match is closed to late joins (participants and sides fixed at the start); a wrecked car gets the free-roam recovery (the original's rule is unrecovered); the original skips parked cars in networked Cops & Robbers (already encoded in `session.rs`) and ambient traffic is Cruise-only. F27-B.4b: the match is built while the session loads (`cnr::start_match`, from the city's site pool; a city that cannot seed a round fails the session) with nobody seated, and `enroll_cnr_participants` seats each non-AI car on the variant's side with the fewest connected members, ties to the first listed (Robbers / Red first) — *designed*: the original lets a player choose a team in its lobby, which is not modelled (team choice is unrecovered), so the split alternates in ascending wire-id order. A participant is keyed by its wire roster id in a networked session (a minted `PlayerId` differs per process, so only the wire id names the same car on host and clients) and by its minted id locally. A participant who left and whose car returns is re-seated on the same side with their points (`GoldMatch::rejoin`; implementation choice — the original's reconnect handling is unrecovered). A client holds no `CnrHost`: it places its markers from the same seeded opening draw, then `sync_cnr_markers` follows the replica. F27-B.4c (CLI leg): `mm2 --cnr ffa|cops|robbers [--cnr-gold weightless|quarter|half] [--cnr-limit none|5m|10m|20m|30m|100pts|250pts|500pts|1000pts]` offers the mode alone (one seat) or with `--host` (advertised, flag-time gated by `validate` + `check_session`); only the host menu's stock choices parse, the spellings are ours (implementation choice). `mm2-host` (headless, no simulation) deliberately does not offer it — it could advertise a match nobody runs. F27-B.4c (HUD leg): `mm2_app::cnrhud` draws a text readout (clock or limit — a countdown rounded up to the second, `first to N` for a point limit, elapsed when unlimited; team totals or the local rank and points; the gold's state and where to take it; the result once decided) from the host's `CnrHost` or a client's `CnrReplica`, so both ends show the host's decision. What a retail HUD *shows* (clock, points, loot holder) follows the documented mode; the layout, wording and dev-font text are *designed* (the original's instrument art and positions are unrecovered). Commentary audio is not part of it. F27-B.4c (menu leg): the main menu's `Cops & Robbers` row → city (disabled with the `check_session` reason when the site pool is short) → options screen (variant, gold weight, limit; cycling over `CnrVariant::ALL`/`GoldMass::ALL`/`MatchLimit::choices`) → `Start match` launches `SessionMode::CopsAndRobbers` single-seat; the screen layout and the variant/`No limit` wording are *designed* (gold-weight and limit names are the `mmlang.dll` strings). A lobby-hosted offer from the menu and commentary remain open, and no two-process run exists. | designed | `mm2_app::cnr`, `mm2_app::cnrnet`, `mm2_net` tests Match-over screen (F27-B.4c rematch leg): designed. A decided match on a `Local`-authority session moves it to `Results` (`cnr::end_decided_match`); the screen lists verdict, reason, played time, team totals and ranked standings (`cnrhud::result_lines`) with `Play again` (the session's own restart: fresh generation, fresh match) and `Continue to menu`. The original's results presentation is unrecovered — wording and layout are ours; nothing is written to the profile (no authored C&R progression). A decided hosted match ends the same way on the host and, from the replicated match (`cnr::end_replicated_match`), on every client; those screens offer only `Back to lobby` because the lobby's `Cancel`/`Start` mint the next match's generation (F27-B.4c rematch-net, designed). Site reach (measured, `docs/research/cnr.md`): only 12/44 sf and 14/46 london authored sites lie on a routable vehicle lane (15 m horizontal / 5 m vertical); where one does, the site `y` is the road + ≈ 2.4 m. Whether the original deliberately places sites off the road is unknown — the data is measured, no rule is claimed. |
| CNR-13 | Gold commentary (F27-B.4c): **wave pools measured, triggers designed.** Each city table (`cnrsf`/`cnrlondon`) names one speaker-qualified prefix per family (`AS1\AS1COPS`, `AL1\AL1ROBROB`, so there is no announcer draw) and the families *partition one wave pool per speaker* through `<end>,<add>`: sf `ROBGETLOOT 3,0` = `as1cops01`–`03`, `COPSTASHLOOT 4,3` = `04`, `COPDROPLOOT 5,4` = `05`, `ROBDROPLOOT 6,5` = `06`, `ROBRECOVERLOOT`/`COPGETLOOT 9,8` = `09`, `ROBSTASHLOOT 10,9` = `10`, `COPRECOVERLOOT 11,10` = `11` (the `ROBROB` team lines 3–6, 9, 10 likewise); the shipped pool is `as1cops01`–`11`/`as1robrob01`–`10` (london `al1cops01`–`12`). So a draw is `add + 1 ..= end` (AUD-12; the previous `add + 1 + rng % end` named waves that do not ship). `MM2_RETAIL` test `retail_every_family_window_names_only_shipped_waves`: all 14 families × both cities resolve every wave in their window through the production `WaveBank`. Triggers (designed; the exe's are unrecovered): a pickup of fresh gold = `GET`, of dropped gold = `RECOVER`, a carrier's loss = `DROP`, a delivery = `STASH`, voiced for the carrier's side — `ROB*`/`COP*` for Cops vs. Robbers, the `RED`/`BLUE` team lines for Robbers vs. Robbers (no authored team *recover*: it reads `…HASGOLD` again), and free-for-all voices the `ROB*` families (it authors none of its own; its players deliver to a hideout). Everyone hears every call (non-spatial, volume 1.0 like the pre-race lines); lines queue three deep, a line waiting past 6 s is dropped counted, 0.25 s apart (all designed). The authority voices its `CnrEvent`s; a client derives the call from the change between replica frames (`GoldView::call_since`) — a lost frame costs a line, never a score, and a client's first frame is a baseline. | verified_original (families, pools, windows); designed (triggers, FFA family, queue) | `aud/spchdata/cnr*.csv` + `mm2-inspect list`; `mm2_content::cnr::commentary_cue`, `mm2_app::cnrvoice` |
| CNR-14 | Cops & Robbers results presentation (F17-AC01): **layout designed, content documented.** The match-over overlay reads the authority's decided `GoldMatch` (or a client's replica) through `cnrhud::result_lines`: verdict (`YOU WIN` / `PLAYER n WINS` / `<SIDE> WIN` / `TIE`), end reason and clock, the team totals line on the team variants only, then every participant best first with side and points, leavers marked. Individual points and team totals follow CNR-4 / UI-5; the wording, ordering, tie rule's presentation and the *Play again* / *Back to lobby* rows are our own — the original's results layout is not recovered, so none of it is claimed as original. The screen mints nothing: points, winner and end reason come from `GoldMatch`. | documented (content) / designed (layout) | tests `mm2_app` `tests/results.rs` `cnr_results::*` (free-for-all, Cops vs. Robbers at the point limit, Robbers vs. Robbers with a leaver, a tie) |

## HUD, map and cameras

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HUD-1 | Dashboard instruments: speedometer, tachometer (shift near redline on manual), gear display (R N 1 2 3 4…), damage meter, steering bar (mouse control only). Asset evidence (2026-09-25): every stock `vp*` ships `geometry/<id>_dash.pkg` — flat quads authored in one shared cluster space (`dash`/`roof`/`speed_needle`/`tach_needle`/`damage_needle`/`gear_indicator`/`wheel` parts) — plus `tune/<id>_dash.asnode` (`DashPos`/`RoofPos`/`WheelPos`, per-gauge `*Offset`/`*PivotOffset`, `*RotMin/Max` sweeps in radians, `WheelFact`) and `tune/camera/<id>_dash.campovcs` (`Offset`/`ReverseOffset`/`TrackTo`/`Pitch`/FOV/near/far). The gear indicator is **not** a moving strip: the dash pkg's paint-job table is repurposed as a gear-slot table — shader 4 of paint job *n* names a per-slot texture, measured `R, N, One…Six, D` identically on vpbug/vpbus/vpmustang99/vpford/vpcaddie — so the engaged gear selects the quad's material (verified data; the runtime selection call is inferred, mmDashView's paint index). Implemented as F22-B.1 (`mm2_formats::dash`/`mm2_app::dash`): authored cluster + roof card + cockpit camera spawned under the player vehicle, needles driven by `*RotMin/Max` sweeps against `Trans.High` top speed and authored redline, damage needle gated on the authored damage record, wheel rolls by `steer_angle/lock × WheelFact`, gear quad rebinds to slot 0 (R) or 2+gear (One…Six→D clamp). Designed readings: `*Offset` composition as position corrections, `N`/`D` trigger conditions (our sim never rests in neutral), `WheelFact` units — DSN-47/UNK-27/UNK-28. | documented (help) + verified (asset layout/gear table) | help:Using Dashboard Instruments; `mm2-inspect pkg --verts`/shader census on retail `geometry/vp*_dash.pkg` 2026-09-25; mm2hook `mmDashView`/`RadialGauge` class layout |
| HUD-2 | Race instruments: green compass arrow (Checkpoint/Blitz), checkpoint list, laps record (Circuit), place indicator, stopwatch (Checkpoint/Circuit), countdown timer (Blitz). F22-A.4 implements the stopwatch/countdown pair (the `mmTimer`s the recovered `mmHUD` layout owns): a session-owned row composes the authored `digitac_*`/`digi_colon` glyph set bound through the VFS (`absent` on the report, never substitute art), driven by `RaceState::time_remaining` for timed definitions and `RaceState::clock` otherwise so it freezes with pause/finish; live through `Countdown` and `Running`, dark on `Complete`/stale generations, gated by `H` like every `mmHUD` member while the report keeps composing; `camera::retarget_hud` pins it to the active world camera with the rest of the layer. The exact original layout is unrecovered — DSN-53/UNK-32. F22-A.5 implements the compass arrow (the `mmArrow` `mmHUD` owns) bound to the authored `hudarrow{01,_blitz01,_cc01}.pkg` chevron + `s_hudarrow_*` tiles — one sprite per paint job rasterized at spawn, the node's `UiTransform` carrying the signed bearing and the image swapping family-tile/yellow-tile across the ±90° line; scope, cycling, phase and `H` gating unchanged from the dev-needle contract. F22-A.6 implements the checkpoint list, laps record and place indicator (`mm2_app::racestat`): a session-owned top-right cluster composes the authored `digitac_*_half` glyph set bound through the VFS (`absent` on the report, never substitute art), reading `RaceProgress` cleared flags/lap, `live_order` for the place, and `navigation_target` for the armed gate so the lit entry always matches the arrow. Research correction: the `texture/race_*` TGA tiles decode fine through `city::load_image` (plain 24bpp TGA 2.0 files, not a masked variant) but visual inspection shows they are menu/results-screen panels ("Laps", "Opponents", "Race Records", "Select Vehicle"), not in-race instrument labels — the instruments bind the half-size digit set and compose their own labels instead. Cluster layout, label text and gate-state tints are a designed reading (DSN-54/UNK-34). | documented | help:Using Dashboard Instruments; `geometry/hudarrow*.pkg` + `texture/s_hudarrow_*.tex` + `texture/digitac_*_half.tga` on retail |
| HUD-3 | Cameras: Chase Near (default), Cockpit, Chase Far on C. W toggles wide-screen; V = "Thrill Cam"; cockpit look left/right/back/forward on numpad 4/6/2/8; BACKSPACE rearview mirror; D dashboard; H HUD; I opponent indicator. Implemented as F22-B.1: `CameraMode::Cockpit` binds the authored `camPovCS` (`Offset` eye, `Pitch`, authored FOV/near/far, `ReverseOffset` for the back-look), numpad 4/6/2/8 drive the authored-anchored look (glance magnitudes designed, DSN-49). F22-B.3 implements the chase pair: every stock `vp*` authors `tune/camera/<id>_{near,far}.camtrackcs` (`camTrackCS` records: `Offset` boom anchor, `TrackTo` aim, `MinDist`/`MaxDist`+`MinMaxOn`, `MinSpeed`/`MaxSpeed` speed window, `CollideType`, FOV/near/far, plus approach/steer/hill dynamics fields — mm2hook `camTrackCS`), bound per vehicle as `ChaseLens`es on the chase camera; `C` now runs the documented Chase-Near→Cockpit→Chase-Far chain marker-driven (Chase-Far skipped when no far record exists) with the dev Free camera appended after it, `CollideType!=0` enables a ray-cast occlusion pull-in, and the speed window extends the boom toward `MaxDist` across `MinSpeed..MaxSpeed` (designed readings — DSN-56/UNK-36). F22-B.4 scopes the `CollideType` occlusion ray to exclude the player's own trailer entities (the authored near boom lands inside a trailered rig's own trailer box — vpsemi/vpcentury verified broken then fixed on windowed captures; UNK-36) and (superseded by the UNK-37 recovery) bound the cockpit near plane to the loader's constant 0.1 m, since the original overwrites the authored `CameraNear` after every load and four `_dash.campovcs` records author an unused 3.0 (UNK-37). F22-B.2 implements the BACKSPACE rearview as a mirror strip: a rearward `MirrorCamera` parented to the player vehicle at the authored `camPovCS` eye (designed fallback when absent), rendering a top-centre viewport strip, toggled only in Playing/Countdown so overlays keep the key, suppressed under the dev Free camera, and excluded from `WorldCamera3d` so it never becomes the listener/PVS/pose source; `RearView` is session-agnostic so a restart respawns the strip armed. `F4` now carries the documented restart binding CTL-1 assigns it (the dev build had borrowed BACKSPACE). The strip's geometry and the original mirror's exact presentation are unrecovered — DSN-50/UNK-29. F22-A.2 implements the `I` opponent indicator: a session-owned marker pool over each non-local `Player` participant (AI opponents today; remote drivers once networking exists), bound per-frame in entity order so a despawned or vehicle-less participant frees its slot the same update and the local car never carries one. Markers bind the authored `geometry/hudmap_tri.pkg` through the VFS like every authored resource (`absent` on the report, never a substitute mesh), stand the flat tri upright apex-down over the opponent's authored collider/`chassis_size` roof, yaw-face the active `WorldCamera3d` camera, and paint from the same authored opponent palette slots the minimap's tri pool cycles (shared `TRI_PAINT_OPPONENTS` — an opponent's arrow matches its map tri). `I` toggles only in `Playing`/`Countdown` so overlays keep the key, and the toggle is session-agnostic so a restart respawns the pool armed. The original's indicator presentation is unrecovered — the documentation records only the toggle — so the arrow shape/extent, the roof lift, the palette mapping and the on-by-default start state are designed readings (DSN-51, UNK-30). F22-A.3 implements the documented `H` HUD toggle: `mmHUD` is recovered (mm2hook) as a single node owning `mmHudMap` (which itself draws the in-world opponent indicators via `DrawIndicator`), `mmArrow`, the stopwatch/countdown `mmTimer` pair, `mmDashView` and `mmCRHUD`, exposing `Enable`/`Disable`/`Toggle` — so `H` is the master gate over the whole driving-HUD layer, not just a telemetry line. `HudVisible` (session-agnostic, on by designed default) suppresses the instrument line, nav arrow, countdown/low-time cues, the map's corner views, the opponent indicators and the cockpit dash cluster while every driver keeps computing and re-shows on re-enable; `H` toggles only in `Playing`/`Countdown`. Outside the layer by design (DSN-52): the mirror strip, the nav debug overlay, `ErrorText`, the pause/results/menu overlays and the full-screen pause map (a menu surface — the corner views still park); whether the original's gate touches the mirror and its exact flag scope stay unrecovered (UNK-31). `--no-hud` starts the layer off for captures (render-only). F22-B.5 adds the transition/reset leg: the chase boom tracks the vehicle's previous pose and snaps to the new anchor when one frame's displacement exceeds `BOOM_SNAP_SPEED × dt` (120 m/s designed — above every authored top speed, below any real teleport), covering `R`/`ResetVehicle` teleports, recoveries and mode re-entry (the tracker goes stale while `chase_follow` is gated out); ordinary motion, the decimetre-scale upright hop and the near↔far lens swap still ease, and a rig's first tracked frame snaps (no history to ease from). `R`'s player+trailer reset bundle is now shared with the scheduled `--reset-at <ticks>` evidence override (record-ineligible), and `--cam-cycle-at <ticks>` walks the same `C` chain once at its session tick (render-only). Original transition semantics stay unrecovered — UNK-36. Deviations: the dev Free cam sits after the documented three-view chain rather than replacing Chase-Far (F22-B.3 repaired the earlier stand-in — DSN-48/DSN-56); the dash toggle moved from `D` (steer-right in the enhanced WASD map) to `V` — the behavior stays, the binding moves (DSN-48). | documented | help:Camera Views, booklet:p9; `tune/camera/*_dash.campovcs` on retail |
| HUD-4 | Map: TAB cycles two smaller map views/off, E zooms, F toggles rotation, Q = full-screen pause map (single player only). Shows player, opponent triangles, un-cleared (bright) / cleared (dark) checkpoints, yellow highlight on arrow target, finish line when unlocked. Exe/asset evidence (2026-09-25): a `mmHudMap` class loads `geometry/hudmap_<city>.pkg` (world-space XZ quads textured `s_*map*` — sf tiles span the city extent, so world→map alignment is identity), `hudmap_{square,tri}` markers authored flat in XZ (tri ~16×28 m nose −Z; square ~14×14 m with `*_DOT` paint jobs), and `IOID_MAP`/`MAPORIENT`/`MAPRES`/`FMAP` controls; `tune/{sf,london}.mmhudmap` is its authored tune (window `Size`/`Pos` fractions, `ZoomIn`/`Approach`/`ZoomInDist`/`ZoomOutDist`, `IconScale{Min,Max}`, fullscreen equivalents, `Ocean` color). Implemented as F22-A.1 (`mm2_formats::hudmap`/`mm2_game::hudmap`/`mm2_app::hudmap`): inset/large/off cycle, authored zoom pair + approach-rate ease, north-up/rotating, fullscreen pause map that replaces the pause overlay, participant/checkpoint/finish markers. The unrecovered parts (second inset view's exact layout, `ZoomIn==0` start semantics, icon-scale application) are designed readings — DSN-46, UNK-26. | documented | help:Displaying a Map of the City; exe `mmHudMap`/`IOID_MAP`/`MAPORIENT`/`FMAP` strings; `geometry/hudmap_*.pkg`, `tune/*.mmhudmap` on the retail install; `mm2-inspect dump` |
| HUD-5 | London tunnels are marked on the map; some alleys/shortcuts are not. | documented | help:Tips for Winning |

| ID | Rule | Class | Source |

| WLD-2 | Pedestrians exist on streets and react audibly (screaming "Maniac!"); can be toggled via Show Pedestrians. | documented | help:Configuring Sound, help:Graphics Options |
| WLD-3 | Documented original defect: vehicles can stick in soft ground under SF dock archways; less likely at speed. | documented | readme:§3.05 |
| WLD-4 | Weather and time-of-day are per-event selectors (0-3 each in data; enum meaning unverified — UNK-1). | verified_original | data:`mm*data.csv` Weather/TimeofDay columns |
| WLD-5 | Sidewalks, alleys, parks, wrong side of road are all drivable shortcuts. | documented | help:Tips for Winning |
| WLD-6 | `.aimap` override grammar is uniform across all 209 retail files: `[Speed Limit]` scalar, counted `[Exceptions]` (road-id/density/speed), `[Police]`/`[Opponent]` spawns, `[Ambient Types/Density]` cumulative weights closing at 1.0, `[Ambients Drive On The Left]` (1 london / 0 sf), ped-model pairs; rarer `[Density]`, `[CopChaseDistance]`, `[AmbientLaneChanges]`, `[Traffic Lights]`, `[Hookmen]`. | verified_original | data: all `city/`+`race/` aimaps, `mm2-inspect aimap`; docs/research/aimap.md |
| WLD-7 | Retail exception density/speed values are all `0.00`/`0` — consistent with R3's claim that event aimaps close course roads to ambient traffic. Runtime consumption of these values is unverified (UNK-12). | inferred | data: every `[Exceptions]` row on retail |
| WLD-8 | Eight london `.aimap` files carry `[Exceptions]` road ids 562–815, outside `city/london.bai`'s 540-road space; SF exceptions are all in range. Different id space or dead authored refs — unknown (UNK-18). | verified_original | `mm2-inspect aimap` cross-check vs `city/london.bai` |
| WLD-9 | BAI section frames carry `x_axis` = `tangent × up` (the geometric right of travel with the sections) on ~all sections of both retail cities; right-side lane curves sit mostly at +x, left-side at −x — exceptions are authored geometry, so lane rank comes from measured offset, not side slot. | verified_original | measured on `city/{sf,london}.bai`; docs/research/bai.md |
| WLD-10 | Ambient turn rules: innermost lane turns toward centre or straight, outermost kerb-side or straight, middle lanes straight; one-way roads may take any outgoing road; freeway ramps may force a right; U-turns never. Intersection road lists are authored counterclockwise. London's left-hand driving is baked into authored BAI data (lane swap + reversed vertex/lane order), not a runtime flag. | documented | Adzima GDMag ambient-AI article; docs/research/bai.md |
| WLD-11 | Each retail city's vehicle road graph is one connected component (SF has exactly one dead end); 166/540 london and 96/379 sf roads are one-way; 176 roads carry no routable vehicle lanes (pedestrian/special/disabled or curve-less). Directed reachability (`mm2-inspect nav --routes`, 2026-09-20): london's graph is strongly connected — all 606 arcs reach all others. SF's is not: beyond the one dead end (road 0 backward), six arcs reach junctions no arm legally departs — authored one-way traps (roads 92/94/97/99 reach only themselves, 102/111 reach 2, road 1 backward reaches 617 of 618) — leaving 4927 ordered arc pairs unreachable (~1.3%); 505/512 seeded route probes succeed, the 7 failures are exactly the trap-region sources. Event `[Exceptions]` bind at routing time: `race/london/blitz0.aimap`'s 8 closures isolate roads 115-/116-/368+/375+ (15016 unreachable pairs, 24/512 probes unreachable — e.g. `270→108` routes 19 steps/1428 m open, `Unreachable` closed) and `race/sf/blitz0.aimap`'s 3 raise unreachable pairs to 7977 (11/512 probes); no probe ever traverses a closed road mid-route. | verified_original | `mm2-inspect nav --routes/--aimap` on retail |
| WLD-12 | `PTH1` pathset grammar is uniform across all 98 parseable retail files: header cursor + named paths of attributed points, kinds 0 single-points / 1 directed-pairs / 2 line-strip, spacing authored in quarter metres (0–60 m range seen; 0 and 5 m dominate). Placement names resolve to `geometry/<n>.pkg` (props) or `texture/<n>.*` (decals); `PATHnn` names are route labels (audio paths, parked-car/ferry/train routes). | verified_original | data: all 101 `.pathset` files, `mm2-inspect pathset`; docs/research/pathset.md |
| WLD-13 | `city/<city>/props.pathset` is the ambient prop-dressing source: london stamps 1033 prop instances from 87 paths (0 unresolved), sf 778 from 113 paths (1188/925 before WLD-33's strip rule) — its remaining 31 paths are `r4i_rails_f` *decal* names living inside the prop file, classified separately not failed. `props.pathset` names are prop PKGs; decal/audio/race pathsets are different consumers. | inferred | `mm2 --city {london,sf}` import reports + rendered stamp rows; docs/research/pathset.md |
| WLD-14 | The PSDL `prop_rule` byte selects a `n{NN}left`/`n{NN}right` row pair in `city/<city>/proprules.csv`: london bytes 1–16 ↔ rules n01–n16, sf 1–20 ↔ n01–n20 (n14 defined but unreferenced); 0 = no roadside props. `propdefs.csv` rows name 1–4 PKG variants plus start/distance/maxUse/minLerp/maxLerp fields; `props.csv` is a `Group,Name` membership list (retail: `Races` only). Each city has exactly one room carrying byte 205 with no matching rules — an authored anomaly, not a parser artifact. | verified_original | `mm2-inspect proprules` on retail; docs/research/proprules.md |
| WLD-15 | `tune/banger/*.dgbangerdata` is one `dgBangerData` record per banger stem (999 on retail, all `type: a`), carrying Size/CG/Mass/Elasticity/Friction/ImpulseLimit2/NumParts plus a `BirthRule` particle spec. The stem→geometry link is by name alone: `geometry/<stem>.pkg` (standalone prop, 216), `geometry/<stem>.mtx` or a matching PKG chunk (named part, 477), `<base>_break<NN>` → the `BREAK<NN>` chunk inside `geometry/<base>.pkg` (254 resolved); `default.dgbangerdata` is the fallback record. On every standalone prop, `NumParts` equals the PKG's distinct `BREAK<NN>` index count (0 mismatches). 47 authored records resolve to no geometry at all. | verified_original | `mm2-inspect banger` on retail; docs/research/banger.md |
| WLD-16 | World bangers bind by placed name (`N` → `tune/banger/<N>.dgbangerdata`) and reach the world through pathset stamping (`props.pathset` 17/17 london, 30/30 sf prop names bound; race overlays all bound) and the prop-rule channel (PSDL `prop_rule` → `proprules.csv` → `propdefs.csv` files, all bound). INST is the static-architecture channel: 0/386 distinct names bound. `*_ai.inst` supplements stamp only bound `sp_stop_f`. Vehicle (`vp*`/`va*`) records bind through the vehicle pipeline — 269/994 records reachable via world placements, 562 vehicle-owned, 163 authored-but-never-placed on retail. | verified_original | `mm2-inspect banger-bind` on retail; docs/research/banger.md |
| WLD-17 | Prop-rule PSDL geometry: a `Psdl::paths` record is one road run between two junction crossings and `road_rooms` chains the road rooms it traverses, consecutive entries sharing direct road-to-road boundaries. `start_crossroads`/`end_crossroads` name the curb vertex pair of the end crossings; a crossing occupies four consecutive perimeter points `[outer, curb, curb, outer]`, and at a direct road-to-road boundary the curb pair is the widest consecutive point pair marked with the neighbouring room id. The two arcs between the entry/exit crossings are the sidewalk building lines. Every rule-bearing room a sane path reaches resolves its crossings under this model (0 misses, both cities); some sf `road_rooms` entries encode non-room values (65 0xx range). | verified_original | measured on `city/{sf,london}.psdl` + `mm2_game::props` runtime walk; docs/research/proprules.md |
| WLD-18 | Decal `LineStrip` paths interleave two authored ribbon edges: even point indices form one edge, odd the other, each (even, odd) pair is a cross-section carrying the strip's width, and consecutive sections join into textured quads. Verified against retail: the pairing produces coherent authored widths — ~1 m zigzag (`decal_zigzag_l`), ~2 m cable-car rail channels (`r4i_rails_f`), ~8 m zebra crossings (`decal_rxwalk03_l`), 15–20 m junction paint (`decal_x_inter_l`) — where a centreline reading yields incoherent alternating ~1 m/~20 m segments; texture contents corroborate the axis (zigzag line oscillates along texture V, zebra bars run along U). Palette alpha in decal P8 textures is authored translucency (unpainted surrounds ≈ 36, painted bars ≈ 240). The exact UV policy (v tiling period vs stretch, u direction) and the original's blend state remain inferred. | verified_original | measured on `city/{sf,london}/decals.pathset` + `texture/decal_*.tex`; docs/research/pathset.md |

The `.anim` frame layout is recovered: channel 0 is the root translation, channels 1..`NumBones` are one Euler rotation triple per bone in `.skel` pre-order, composed in the AGE `Rx·Ry·Rz` order (`EulerRot::XYZEx` — verified by the standing pose's mirrored arm pairs, root-Z travel matching `Y AXIS DISTANCE`, and the dive clips' end poses landing prone aligned with travel); the csv `OFFSET`/`DISTANCE` columns are measured forward/lateral per-window travel bookkeeping (mm2hook's `pedAnimationSequence` `FSpeed`/`LSpeed`). The authored `tri` winding is the front face (941 of 963 bind-pose triangles agree with the authored normals; `mm2-inspect peds`).
