| PED-2 | Car-versus-pedestrian contact (F19-B.8, 2026-10-11): **no physical interaction is evidenced** in `Midtown2.exe`, but the car side was not traced to exhaustion. Read: the walker update `0x54b9a0` has a reaction word of 0 walking / 1 wary / 2 diving only and its sole input from a car is the sensing of UNK-43 (distance, speed, corridor timing, steering side) — no branch reads a contact, impact or overlap, and no state exists for being struck. The executable ships no string for a hit, splat, roadkill, damage or score on a pedestrian (`strings` sweep: ped strings are the AI-map creation errors, `PEDESTRIAN DENSITY`, `pedpool`, the `{f,m}pedvoice` sound loaders, `Ped %d biffed.` and `pedRagdollMgr`). `Ped %d biffed.` (`0x544193`) is the exception-handler arm of the per-ped update loop (the same arm prints `Vehicle/Opp/Cop/Hookman %d biffed.` for every other actor loop) — a fault report, not a hit. `pedRagdollMgr` is a singleton check in the constructor at `0x57b630` (global `0x6b3720`, vtable `0x5b535c`); the object is the ped animation state-graph player (it steps a 36-byte-stride state table by `±` time deltas and moves to the next state at a threshold, `0x57b070`/`0x57b2d0`), i.e. the csv ground-recovery chain, not evidence of physics ragdolls. The walker instance is an `lvlInstance`-style object (vtable `0x5b490c`, `GetSize` 0x3c, constructor `0x551b50`). **Car-side broad phase traced (2026-10-12, #1302): walkers are not filtered out.** `dgPhysManager` calls `GatherCollidables` `0x4694d0` per declared mover (`DeclareMover` `0x468360`, flag word `0x1b` at every vehicle call site); the gather keeps an instance of the per-room list (`0x654d7c`, `MoveToRoom` `0x465470`) when vtable slot +0x50 (`0x43fc30`, `[inst+8]` bit 4) or +0x54 (`0x43fc40`, `[inst+9]` bit 0) is true and a sphere test passes. The base constructor `0x4631e0` leaves bit 4 clear but the walker constructor sets `or byte [this+8], 0xd8` (`0x551d01`), so the walker passes; the walker's `0x5b52f0` sub-instance (slot +0x50 = `0x57b500`, always false) does not. The narrow phase `0x469610` needs a non-null vtable +0x64 object on both sides; **that guard resolves non-null for the walker** (2026-10-12, #1354). Walker slot +0x64 is `0x552d00` = `[0x553d40(this)+0xb8]`; `0x553d40` returns `[0x6b218c] + 0x18 + idx*0xbc` (`[0x6b218c]` is the `aiVehicleManager` singleton, vtable `0x5b499c`; `idx` is the byte at walker-instance +5, stored by the instance constructor `0x551b50` from `0x553d60`'s result at `0x551d1e`). That record (32-entry inline array, stride `0xbc`, ctor `0x56f4f0`, vtable `0x5b4c20`) gets +0xb8 written exactly once, in the get-or-create `0x553d60`: after `[record]` slot +0x28 (`0x4a1110`) loads the model's data file, `0x553f3e` stores a freshly allocated+constructed 0x318-byte bounds object (operator new `0x5770e0`, ctor `0x46a260`, vtable `0x5b0d98`). Nothing clears it — the record destructor `0x554160`/`0x4a0cf0`, the record's own methods (`0x56f4f0`..`0x56fb84`, all reads), the manager reset loop `0x553fa0`, the manager methods `0x553200`/`0x553470` and all record base methods only read it; the sole other writer is the same function's allocation-failure branch (OOM only). A model the loader rejects (`No file for '%s' for vehicle data`, `0x5d76bc`) is blacklisted and returns 0, leaving `[instance+5]=0`, which indexes record 0 — created by the first successful load — so the field is non-null in any scene that loaded a vehicle model. **The guard therefore does not short-circuit the walker: the retail narrow phase runs on walkers and yields a car-walker contact when the shapes overlap.** The only car-side reader of the `[inst+9] \|= 0x80` contact mark is `0x55b154`, which sets a car field (+0x153c) to 2.0 when set, 1.0 otherwise (a car response, not a walker one). Consequently: *inferred from static code* that retail cars collide with walkers; the *response* stays unknown (no walker-side struck state, hit/splat/score string, or animation change is evidenced). The port now implements the contact as a *designed* bounded-contact body (DSN-89, F19-B.8b, 2026-10-13, #1378): each walker gets a kinematic torso capsule so a car that catches it is blocked at the body with no interpenetration, while the walker plays only the authored stop/look/dive chain — no struck state, animation, score or sound, and the car-side response field `0x55b154`/`+0x153c` is left as measured. Ruled out: a ped hit state, hit/score strings, a `biffed` hit message, and a broad-phase filter. Open: OWNER play observation (drive into a walker in a Cruise city and describe blocking, knock-down, sound, score). | inferred | `Midtown2.exe` 0x54b9a0, 0x544193, 0x57b630/0x57b070, 0x551b50, vtable 0x5b490c; 0x552d00, 0x553d40, 0x553d60/0x553f3e, 0x46a260, 0x469610/0x469a0b, 0x554160/0x4a0cf0, 0x55b154; docs/research/pedanim.md "Car contact" |

| WLD-36 | Shallow-water bound polygons resolve their named material through the city material manager. Hyde Park's placed lake has 39 `water` polygons: drag 0.119, friction 0.68, particle pair -1/6 at thresholds 0/0. Wheel drag opposes contact-plane velocity quadratically, scaled by tire drag coefficient and normal load. Wheel particle thresholds compare strictly against `vehWheel+0x220` visual slip; emission accumulates `InitialBlast × dt × suspension-load factor`, retaining fractional credit (the splash rule has SpewRate 0). Emitter origin trails the wheel by a radius and rises with load-scaled Radius (also setting particle size); velocity components scale with lateral, rolling and slip speeds in the contact basis. | verified_original | Kuna v1.785 on retail `Midtown2.exe`: bound loader `0x48579b`, manager `0x466a40`, wheel contact `0x4d29f0`, wheel forces `0x4d34d0`, particles `0x4d1e80`/`0x4d1ee0`; data:`bound/kl_hydeparklake_l_bound.bnd`, `city/materials.mtl`; docs/research/shallow-water.md |
| WLD-37 | A static INST prop collides with its authored ASCII bound (`bound/<name>_bound.bnd`, prop-local frame, placed by the INST basis), not its render mesh. The render mesh carries planes that are not solid: Golden Gate's `pl_ggbridge_*_mid_f` `bot_f` deck-undersides stand 0.21–0.25 m proud of the PSDL road across ~120 m of deck, and a car crossing the seam at z ≈ −1183 / −1273 left the road at 4–8 m/s vertical (#1443); tower bridge's `cl_towerbrdge01_rt_l` has a 0.33 m one. The bound has no such plane (≤ 0.15 m anywhere on the GG deck). The frame is measured, not assumed: the `pl_ggbridge_*_upper*` bounds (render mesh a one-triangle placeholder) land exactly on the visible tower legs of the `*_side_f` meshes (x −1790.6/−1770.1, z −1890…−1882). Stamped (pathset/prop-rule) props keep PKG collision — their content offset is measured for the mesh, not the bound — as do props whose bound is absent (79 of 165 SF INST names), binary or malformed. Per-polygon bound `friction`/`elasticity` stay unmodelled (every GG bound is 0.5/0.1 `default`). | inferred | `bound/` census of retail `sf.inst`/`london.inst` names (86/165 SF, 206/221 London have an ASCII bound); `tests/golden_gate.rs` (downward rays over the loaded GG deck: 522 samples above the road with the mesh, 0 with the bound); `docs/research/placement.md` § Golden Gate deck |

The DLS wave pools hold 368 waves, **all uncompressed mono 16-bit PCM** (8 000–44 100 Hz, 357 s) and every region→wave link and pool cue lands — so the sample data needs no new codec; what is missing is the DirectMusic *performance* layer (style/pattern note generation, tempo/command tracks, band→DLS instrument mapping, mixing). | verified_original (container structure, reference closure, wave format), inferred (`repeats` as a loop count), unknown (track payload meaning, segment transitions, tempo/groove command semantics) | `mm2-inspect audio` on retail; `mm2_formats::dmus`; docs/research/audio.md |
| AUD-15 | Pedestrian contextual audio (F19-B.9, 2026-10-11): retail ships **ped voice tables** — `aud/creaturedata/default_{f,m}pedvoice<N>.csv`, each authoring one speed band `0,500,0.01,0` of `*SCREAM*` samples (AUD-16 grammar) — and `Midtown2.exe` loads them by name (`%s_fpedvoice%s%d`/`%s_mpedvoice%s%d` at file offsets 0x1d6bd8–0x1d6c24, counts from `numfemalepedvoicefiles`/`nummalepedvoicefiles` at 0x1c2c84/0x1c2c9c). So scream-class ped voices exist in retail. **Traced with Ghidra 10.4 (F19 #1320):** the two loaders are `0x53cd70` (female) and `0x53cde0` (male); each has exactly one caller, the walker constructor `0x54b0b0`, which loads `default` once per walker into a per-walker audio emitter embedded at walker+0xc0 (position pointer = walker+0x60, near/far 0 / 40.0 m, `+0x100 = 8`; class vtable `0x5b3c9c`). The walker update `0x54b9a0` calls only that emitter's per-frame slot `0x510930`, which releases the sample instance once it has stopped; the reaction states 1/2 never call an audio routine. **Activation recovered (F19 #1345):** the start is not the per-frame slot 3 but the emitter method `0x510cd0(arg)` (`if emitter+0x64 != -1 { 0x512370(); if emitter+0x44 != -1 { 0x5128e0(arg) } }`, where `0x512370`/`0x512210` allocate a sound-manager instance index into emitter+0x44 and call the virtual activate slot `0x510980`). `0x510cd0` is called only from the walker animation-transition code, with `this = walker+0xc0`, at four sites: `0x54cd90` (reached from the walker update `0x54b9a0` and from `0x54d0e0`) when the current animation is `BACKUP`, `0x54d8b0` (reached from `0x54b9a0`) in the branch that turns the walker to face away and enters the `0x5d7288` animation, and — with `arg = EBX` (decompiled as 0, not traced) — twice in `0x54d8b0` right after it selects a dive animation (`LDIVE`/`RDIVE`/`ANTIC_*DIVE`/`WALK_*DIVE`). So retail starts the scream when a walker begins a dive or backs away, not on the reaction word itself. **Still not recovered:** what the speed band (`0,500`) gates (`0x5128e0` loops the table's bands with the in-range test `0x514f30`, then `0x514c90` picks the sample with a random draw that avoids repeating the previous pick `DAT_006b0558`; the `arg` is not used by the decompiled loop), the numbered-file choice (`0x50fa90` builds the file-name suffix from a rolling counter) and whether the sound manager (`DAT_006b0474`, audio-enabled flag at +0x25) drops the start. No footsteps or chatter table is evidenced. The port now keys a one-shot to the dive/back-away transitions (a designed binding — trigger mapping, gender pick, numbered-file choice and sample draw are the port's, DSN-109). | inferred | `mm2-inspect audio` creaturedata census; `strings -t x Midtown2.exe`; docs/research/pedanim.md "Pedestrian audio" |
| AUD-16 | `aud/creaturedata` ships 21 plain-text (CRLF) CSVs in two grammars (`mm2_formats::creaturedata`; every sample name resolves to a shipped wave — `mm2-inspect audio` reports 21 supported, 0 unsupported, 0 missing wave ref(s), retail 2026-10-11). *Voice tables* (17 files) — `default_{f,m}pedvoice<N>` (6), `default_ambcarvoice_{l,s}<N>` (8) and the per-vehicle `va_<vehicle>_ambcarvoice_{l,s}` (3) — are a sequence of blocks, each a header line, a numeric row, a `sample name,volume[,play after seconds]` header and sample rows `<stem>,<volume>[,<play after>]` (trailing empty cells are authored padding). A block headed `Min speed,Max speed,min time in range,max time out of range` carries `<min>,<max>,<in>,<out>` — the ped tables author one `0,500,0.01,0` band of `*SCREAM*` samples, the `default_ambcarvoice_*` tables a `0,20,5,0` and a `50,999999,0.25,5` band and the `va_*` tables their own (`0,20,20,0`, `35,999999,0.25,5`; `999999` reads as "unbounded"), every ambient-car table an impact band (retail `min impact force` `5500`) whose sample rows add `play after seconds`. *Count files* (4) — `num{male,female}pedvoicefiles`, `numambcarvoicefiles_{l,s}` — carry a `Num files` heading and one integer; the counts are not the shipped `default_*` inventory (e.g. `nummalepedvoicefiles` is 1 beside three `default_mpedvoice*`), so they are not read as one. The exe names the ped files (`%s_fpedvoice%s%d`/`%s_mpedvoice%s%d`) and the port reads the two `default_{f,m}pedvoice1` tables for the pedestrian scream (AUD-15/DSN-109); the ambient-car tables have no runtime consumer. Runtime semantics stay unrecovered: the speed and force units, what "min time in range"/"max time out of range" gate, how a sample is drawn and the numbered-file selection (UNK-25). | verified_original (schemas, file census, wave resolution), unknown (runtime semantics) | `mm2-inspect audio` on retail; `mm2_formats::creaturedata`; docs/research/audio.md |

## Deliberate rust-mm2 departures (designed, not original claims)

| ID | Policy | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DSN-1 | Four-wheel imported cars use the reconstructed original wheel, drivetrain, engine, aero, gyro and human steering model (PHY-1..PHY-19). The application chooses a fixed 60 Hz sample of the original variable-step simulation; Avian supplies six contact/pose substeps, adapted chassis bounds/contact coefficients and optional flip recovery. The synthetic dev car and trailers keep their configurable model. Formula fidelity is distinguished from measured trajectory evidence. | docs/vehicle-handling.md; tools/handling/README.md; `mm2_vehicle::original`, `original_step` |
| DSN-2 | Rust/Bevy/Avian stack, wgpu rasterized rendering — a modern engine, not a Direct3D emulation; original renderer options (CTL-4) inform features, not implementation. | PROJECT.md, docs/architecture.md |
| DSN-3 | Modern engine-to-engine multiplayer only; no DirectPlay/Zone/serial/modem compatibility. | PROJECT.md |
| DSN-4 | VFS reads installs read-only with deterministic mod overrides. | PROJECT.md, docs/modding.md |
| DSN-5 | Shared race runtime defaults not pinned by authored data: checkpoint vertical band ±8 m, direction-check flag off, 3 s start countdown at 60 Hz. Provisional until real event evidence exists. | `mm2_game::race` constants + docs |
| DSN-6 | Event start without authored `_strtpnts`: the player spawns on the start line facing the row0→row1 tangent — the line is the one point the authored data guarantees is on the course (a 10 m tangent back-off spawned london `blitz6` past the edge of its elevated start deck, over a void; changed 2026-09-20). Checkpoint markers are translucent orange columns, finish a green column — dev-rig visuals, not the original gate rendering. | `mm2_content::race_def`, `mm2_app::race` |
| DSN-7 | Runtime deadline semantics: `TimeLimit` binds as seconds → 60 Hz ticks on Blitz rows only (provisional, UNK-4); the constant values on Checkpoint/Circuit rows stay unbound. The deadline is inclusive — a finish crossing on the expiry tick counts because segments evaluate before the timeout check; expiry records one authoritative `TimedOut` result per unresolved participant. Remaining time displays `m:ss` from the same race clock. | `mm2_content::race_def`, `mm2_game::race`, `mm2_app::race` |
| DSN-8 | Navigation-arrow presentation (RACE-6): the authored `hudarrow*` package is rasterized once to validate its ahead/behind paint bindings. A designed bevelled 3D extrusion uses their average opaque colours and turns around Y from the ground-plane bearing, rendered through an isolated orthographic camera into a transparent top-centre HUD image. Geometry depth, faceted shading, camera angle, canvas size and placement are designed (UNK-33). The scene camera is excluded from world listener/PVS/HUD targeting. It remains hidden under `Ordered` rules per HUD-2 and aims at the armed finish once every gate clears (inferred — RACE-6 names only checkpoints). Cycle keys are X/Z; the enhanced WASD map reserves S for braking. | `mm2_game::race` nav functions; `mm2_app::navarrow`, `mm2_app::navarrow3d` (2026-10-03) |
| DSN-9 | Low-time warning cue: no documented original rule describes a Blitz low-time warning (HUD-2 lists only the countdown timer among race instruments), so the HUD pulses a `LOW TIME` banner once `time_remaining` reaches 10 s at the race tick rate, alternating bright/dim every 0.5 s of remaining time — derived from the same race ticks that judge the deadline, so it freezes with a pause and clears on resolution. Presentation policy, not an original-behavior claim; an audio cue stays impossible until F07 exists. | `mm2_app::race` `LOW_TIME_TICKS`/`update_race_warning` |
| DSN-10 | Banger runtime slice (F04-A.3 + F04-B.1): bound pathset stamps become one session-owned entity (collider + dormant `Banger` + `ObjectIdentity`/`AuthorityRole`, mesh parts as children) instead of the loose render/collider pair. `activate_bangers` compares the squared contact impulse `|K⁻¹·v_rel|²` ((N·s)²) against `ImpulseLimit2` — the quantity OPR-1.3 recovered from `Midtown2.exe` (`0x46b7c0`): `K` is the striker's effective-mass matrix at the contact lever, `v_rel` its approach velocity (manifold normal speed on contacts; bound surface velocity on `StrikeBound` overlaps). A 1000 kg striker crosses an illustrative `Mass 1000 × 85342` prop at ~9.2 m/s head-on (the earlier `½·m·v²` stand-in needed ~410 m/s and never activated the top rungs; retail census: 975 of 995 records reachable at ≤60 m/s, 15 `≥1e30` immovable). Recovered in #1180 (`docs/research/banger.md`): a sub-limit contact attaches then *releases* the active entity (`0x469962` → element slot `+0x1c` = release `0x440020`) and the banger branch skips the ordinary response entirely, so the prop keeps its pose and stays dormant; the striker's `K` is the first-pass matrix. Recovered in #1343: no other pass of the pair step answers a sub-limit hit (`0x4692d0` is a bool filter; `0x475af0`/`0x474a60` only detect; `0x4714d0` is reached only through the skipped block), so a dormant prop is a `Sensor` in the port and the striker feels no response — verified_original for `CollideInstances`, per-vehicle probes against props unread. Not recovered (UNK-22): the tangential part of `v_rel` on the contact path. `NumParts` bounds the fragment loop when > 0 (`0x442442`). A qualifying edge flips `RigidBody` to dynamic once, replays the contact as a reduced-mass two-body transfer (the solver's static wall impulse is returned and `(1+e)·v·μ` paid instead — F04-C.4, implementation choice under UNK-22) plus a `Size`-derived spin kick, and emits `BangerStateChanged`; a ×32 `BangerPool` (the R4-recovered pool size) reclaims oldest-first; a slept body settles back to `RigidBody::Static` (`dgHitBangerInstance`-like, terminal for the session — the `Timer` despawn constants (6.0/30000.0, flag `+0x2a8a8`) sit behind a flag the image only ever stores as 0, so there is nothing to implement). A placement carrying collidable `BREAK<NN>` chunks instead goes `Broken` at the same activation edge (provisional timing, UNK-22): parent collider + mesh children are removed and each piece spawns as its own `Active` fragment body (own `ObjectId`, own `<name>_break<NN>` record or the parent def, convex collider, render children) claiming pool slots — one `BangerStateChanged` per logical break. Intact bound bangers use closed convex PKG hulls for finite dynamic inertia, including open/double-sided meshes; unbound/INST static props keep authored triangle meshes. This collision policy is designed, not recovered original shape selection (UNK-22). Predicted sessions never transition banger state. `BirthRule`, audio/flash/decal effects and replication stay deferred; prop-rule placements now stamp through the same dormant-banger path as pathset stamps (WLD-17/UNK-21). | `mm2_game::banger`, `mm2_app::banger`, `docs/research/banger.md` |
| DSN-11 | Results flow (UI-5, documented): the *local* participant's terminal resolution ends the playing session — `advance_race` transitions `Playing → Results` on the same step it records the `Finished`/`TimedOut` result. The rest of the field races on behind the results screen: `Session::field_races` (`Playing` or `Results`) keeps `advance_race`'s stepping, the race clock, the opponent driver and ambient traffic live in `Results`, so each opponent still records its real finish time (or times out on a Blitz deadline) and the overlay redraws as it lands. Systems serving the local driver — input, damage outcomes, the session clock, the snapshot stream — stay `Playing`-only. Before this, the race froze with the phase and every opponent behind the local driver stayed "still racing" forever. Whether the retail game keeps the field racing after the player finishes is unverified. A remote/AI participant resolving while the local driver still races changes nothing (AC03). Networked reconciliation (report 6 follow-up 2): a hosted session's `Playing → Results` waits for every wire seat (`advance_race`), but `field_races` already covers `Playing`, so the simulated field races and records real finish times through that hold exactly as behind a single-player results screen — one rule, with the wire deferral only delaying the *phase edge* the snapshot stream needs (`the_field_keeps_racing_through_the_wire_deferral`). A joined client reaches `Results` on its own seat's replicated terminal edge, not the host's. `RacePhase::Complete` still waits for every participant to resolve; the Results phase surfaces outcome + place + recorded finish time on the HUD line (place per DSN-12) and, since F17-B.2, a session-owned results overlay presents the local outcome, the generation-scoped standings (unresolved participants listed as still racing, never placed), granted rewards, the persistence disposition from `SessionReport`, and Continue/Restart rows. | `mm2_app::race::advance_race`, `mm2_app::opponents`, `mm2_app::main::update_hud`, `mm2_app::results` |
| DSN-12 | Standings ordering (`ResultLedger::standings`): `Finished` outranks `TimedOut` regardless of times, `Finished` orders by the recorded `race_ticks`, and equal ticks order by `PlayerId` — an explicit, deterministic tie-break independent of recording/query order (no verified original placing rule exists; F14 spec demands an explicit tie policy). A participant with no recorded result is unplaced, not ranked last; DNF `TimedOut` ranks below every finish. The HUD shows `FINISHED {ord}[ of {n}]` from this ordering and the smoke record gains `place=`. | `mm2_game::result`, `mm2_app::main::update_hud`, `mm2_app::smoke` |
| DSN-13 | Live running order (`live_order`) — the place indicator's ordering while a race runs (HUD-2 names the instrument; no verified original rule describes it): `Finished` participants lead ordered by their recorded `race_ticks` (same key as DSN-12's standings, so the live order converges to it as everyone resolves), then active participants by progress — `Ordered` counts `(lap, next)`, `AnyOrder` counts cleared gates — progress ties break toward whoever is closer (straight-line XZ) to their *own* current objective (`checkpoints[next]` / `navigation_target`'s nearest remaining gate or armed finish), `TimedOut` trails by `race_ticks`, and every remaining tie orders by `PlayerId`. Straight-line distance is a heuristic, not course distance; results never consume this order — the ledger owns them. The HUD shows `{ord} of {n}` only with ≥2 participants (a place indicator is a competitive instrument); the smoke record reports `pos=` whenever the local participant has a standing. | `mm2_game::race::live_order`, `mm2_app::main::update_hud`, `mm2_app::smoke` |
| DSN-14 | Opponent bounded re-anchor (F15-B.3): an opponent that spends 540 driving steps (9 s — past one straight back-up and two swings, DSN-113; the scripted player keeps 900) without 8 m of displacement — penned, beached on its hull, or knocked somewhere its route cannot be regained — teleports through the production `ResetVehicle` path back to where its guide last had it (`NeuralState::arc` — a car's place on its guide moves only as the car drives, DSN-45, so this is never further along the route than the car drove), walked backward along the route (4 m clearance, further while inside an un-cleared trigger, on ground that will not carry the car, or within 8 m of another car or another landing claimed the same step; ≤60 m total). **A second re-anchor within 30 m of guide of the last lands ahead instead** (2026-10-14): what stopped the car is still on its line — the settled wreck of a parked car is a static body, and a car put down behind it drove into it again, 39 times in one `london race8` — so the landing is the first of 12/18/25/35/50 m further on where the car can stand, no other car does, and the next 10 m are open at bumper height; the guide between counts as driven, so its gates are not left uncleared. That is the one case in which a re-anchor takes a car along its route, and it needs fixed ground under every 4 m of the way: a car waiting at a raised drawbridge is not put down on the far bank. **A car that is moving but going nowhere is re-anchored too**: 1200 steps (20 s) without getting further along its guide than it has ever been — knocked a street off its line, where its place on the guide stands still (DSN-45), it circled the block and was never still long enough to count as stuck. The jump is *disclosed*: `Teleported` breaks the swept segment so it cannot sweep checkpoints, the car's place on its guide goes back with it so it banks no route arc, `OpponentDriver::reanchors` counts each assist, and the smoke record surfaces the field total as `opp_rec=`. Authority role only — a predicted client never teleports a participant. The original has no such teleport (verified 2026-10-04, docs/research/opponent-ai.md): its stuck detector puts the car into a backup manoeuvre (ours too — DSN-113) and a car that manoeuvre cannot free, or that falls through the world, simply stays out of the race. This re-anchor is a designed anti-standstill policy that departs from the original. | `mm2_app::opponents` (`REANCHOR_*`, `reanchor_pose`, `opponent_drive`), `mm2_app::neural::drive` (`NeuralState::reseat`), `mm2_app::smoke` |
| DSN-15 | Profile store (F16-A.1): our own versioned JSON save format — no compatibility with the retail `players/*.sav`/`*.cfg` binaries is claimed (F16 non-goal). One `<id>.json` per profile under an OS user-data dir (`~/Library/Application Support/rust-mm2/profiles` macOS, `%APPDATA%\rust-mm2\profiles` Windows, `$XDG_DATA_HOME`/`~/.local/share` other Unix); original installs stay read-only. `driver-<n>` ids allocate from a persisted `next-id` high-water mark (advanced before the new profile's first save, floored at the highest surviving file suffix) so a deleted id is never reused; display names may repeat (the id is the identity). Saves are atomic — tmp write + `sync_all`, rotate `.bak`, rename + directory fsync — and `load` keeps the highest surviving `revision` across main/`.tmp`/`.bak`, reporting `recovered_from_backup`; corrupt files still `list()` by id and are never deleted by a read. `version` is stamped on every write and any mismatch rejects the file rather than guessing; unknown top-level fields round-trip through a preserved `extra` map. Progress keys are `EventKey{city, table, stem}` — the authored file stem, not the table row index — so a mod inserting a row cannot retarget a saved record. `ProfileKind::Sandbox` gates `records_progress()`, the single check the F16-B reward path must make. DRV-7 is enforced at the store: the last remaining profile cannot be deleted. | `mm2_game::profile` |
| DSN-16 | Progression application (F16-B.1): `race/<city>/<city>_rewards.csv` normalizes into a session `RewardTable` — `{blitz,circuit,race},half|all` milestone rows (`race` = Checkpoint, the authored token) measured against the catalog's authored family sizes, and indexed `<family>,N` rows bound to the event whose stem is `<prefix><N>` (`crash,3` → `crash3`; the binding is family-aware — an indexed `race,N` row would attach to `race<N>`). `VariantNum` 0 unlocks `vehicle:<id>`, nonzero `paint:<id>:<variant>` where the variant is the zero-based paint index — measured on retail (VEH-4): vpvwcup's variant 5/6 land on "Team Angel"/"Team MS", the documented Angel/Microsoft Cup paints, and every other authored variant lands on its car's declared `Colors` index. An event counts as *beaten* when a `Finished` result meets the documented place criterion (top-3 Amateur, 1st Professional — RACE-3/CHK-3/VEH-3/VEH-4); a solo event finishes at place 1, so both ranks reduce to "finish". `EventRecord` persists `finishes`, `best_race_ticks`, `best_place`, and per-rank `beaten` flags — milestones and indexed rewards read the flags, so repeat finishes and re-delivered results never re-grant (the `unlocks` set is idempotent, F16-AC02) and a record a hand-edited file lost re-grants on the next beaten finish. Results apply only through `record_session_results` draining the authoritative `ResultLedger` — never UI navigation (spec req 3) — into a bound standard profile; sandbox identities (`records_progress()` false) skip it entirely. `record_eligibility` extends DRV-6's default-conditions rule: dev-world sessions, the synthetic dev car, gameplay-affecting `DevOverrides` (`vehicle-config`, `spawn`, `banger-pool`, `traction` ≠ 1.0, `finish` — the presentation-only `--cam`/`--nav`/`--pause` are not counted) and any mounted mod (`SessionConfig.mods_active`, a conservative stand-in until F29 classifies per-mod impact) make every result ineligible, and a `--bot`/`ScriptedDrive` finish never banks progress — the scripted driver is an evidence tool, not the player. `TimedOut` results record nothing. Event *availability* (CHK-2/CHK-3's set-of-three gating, CC-3's lesson→midterm→final chain, RACE-3's per-race customization) is derived state over the same `beaten` flags — DSN-17's `AvailabilityTable`; the menu that enforces it is F17. `vpmoonrover` has no reward row (UNK-3 stands) and Pro points (DRV-4/UNK-8) are still unimplemented. Retail audit (`mm2-inspect events`, 2026-09-21): each city normalizes to 4 event-bound + 6 milestone rules over Blitz=10 Checkpoint=12 Circuit=10 CrashCourse=13 authored rows with zero unresolvable rows. | `mm2_game::progression`, `mm2_content::rewards`, `mm2_app::progression` |
| DSN-17 | Event availability (F16-B.2): `AvailabilityTable` is derived state over the persisted `beaten` flags — one row per authored catalog event, evaluated per query, never stored. Gates: Checkpoint rows group into authored-order sets of three — set 0 open (CHK-2), each later set `AfterAll` the previous set (CHK-3); Crash Course rows read the authored `Description` tag (`lesson<N>`/`midtrm<N>`/`final…`, CC-2) — lessons open, `midtrm<N>` gates `lesson{3N-2}…{3N}` (the tag arithmetic matches the authored order; the tag→group binding is a designed reading of authored labels), `final` gates every midterm (CC-3); Blitz/Circuit rows carry no authored gating. RACE-3's per-event customization is the same `is_beaten` flag on the event's own record (CC-4's passed-lesson any-vehicle replay rides it for the crash family). Designed edges, all fail-open + diagnosed: an unreadable crash tag, a midterm with no lesson group, a final with no midterms stay available and land in `diagnostics`. A sandbox profile evaluates to the unrestricted view (spec req 5's dev access). Until F17's menu enforces selection, a `--event` launch of a locked event runs but warns with the un-beaten prerequisites — the CLI is a dev act, not selection. Retail audit (`mm2-inspect events`, install `fnv1a64:e91e6cd4b2ae30d9`): both cities 32 open / 13 gated / 0 diagnostics — `race3-5 ← race0-2`, `race6-8 ← race3-5`, `race9-11 ← race6-8`; `crash3 ← crash0-2`, `crash7 ← crash4-6`, `crash11 ← crash8-10`, `crash12 ← crash3,crash7,crash11`. | `mm2_game::progression` (`AvailabilityTable`), `mm2_content::availability`, `mm2_app::progression::EventRewards` |
| DSN-18 | Vehicle/paint selectability (F16-B.3): `GarageTable` is derived state over the persisted `unlocks` set — one row per catalog entry, evaluated per query, never stored. Gates come from the authored reward tables, not `.info` `UnlockScore`/`UnlockFlags` (VEH-5 shows those don't correlate — kept verbatim on the row for UNK-6 audit only). A `vehicle:<id>` grant (authored `VariantNum` 0) gates the car; a `paint:<id>:<variant>` grant gates that paint index — variant = the zero-based `Colors` index, measured (DSN-16). A locked vehicle reports every paint locked; unlocking the car does not open its gated paints (each needs its own grant). Every city's reward table feeds the union — a London unlock opens in SF's garage too. Roster membership is the canonical `tune/<id>.info` scan (the original's vehicle list; designed reading): fallback-extension (`.inf`/`.vinfo`/`.info.bak`) or metadata-less entries — vpmoonrover's `.inf` is the retail case, UNK-3 — are `listed=false` but still evaluate. Grants for uncatalogued ids or out-of-range variants land in `diagnostics`, never dropped (F16-AC05's accounting). A sandbox profile evaluates to the unrestricted view. Until F17's garage enforces it, a locked/unlisted `--car` or remembered selection launches but warns (same interim as the locked `--event` warn). | `mm2_game::progression` (`GarageTable`), `mm2_content::garage`, `mm2_app::profile::vehicle_gate_note` |
| DSN-19 | Start-countdown presentation (F17-B.3): a session-owned centered banner shows `ceil(remaining / RACE_TICK_HZ)` — one second per digit — while `RacePhase::Countdown` runs, then `GO!` for `COUNTDOWN_GO_TICKS` (1 s) of the race clock after release. No documented original rule pins the cue's look (DSN-5's 3 s countdown is itself a provisional default; HUD-2 lists no start-cue instrument), so digits/GO/position/duration are a presentation policy, not an original-behavior claim. Deriving both halves off the race state keeps them deterministic: the digits track the countdown's own `remaining` ticks and `GO!` is a `Playing`-phase flash measured in race-clock ticks, so it cannot linger under the pause or results overlays even when the frozen clock still sits inside the window. A zero-length countdown shows only `GO!`. | `mm2_app::race` (`CountdownBanner`, `update_countdown_banner`, `COUNTDOWN_GO_TICKS`) |
| DSN-20 | Stuck detection and recovery (F05-B.2): the authored `vehstuck` bounds drive a designed interpretation of MM2Hook's recovered `vehStuck` struct (its `Update()` body was unrecovered when this was written, so the test combination is ours; the original's is now recovered — DMG-7, PHY-10 — and differs): an `ImpactEvent` anchors the detector at the car's pose (`m_LastImpactPos`), `pos_thresh`/`move_thresh` form a hysteresis band around it (uniform `move > pos` on retail), rotation past `turn` re-anchors the settle window as still-tumbling, and `time_thresh` accrued inside `pos_thresh` fires one bounded `StuckEvent`. Recovery is an in-place upright `ResetVehicle` (heading kept, hull dropped onto the resting surface — `Rotation` 0 and `Translation` ≈ 0.1 on every retail record author no positional rescue or yaw change), trailers re-seated at authored offsets; the reset marks `Teleported` so it cannot sweep a checkpoint. `Rotation`/`Translation` decode verbatim but are not consumed. Remote participants' detectors belong to their authority; `Disabled` wrecks belong to the damage outcome. The authored `TimeThresh` also supplies `assists.self_right_delay` for the pre-existing modern self-righting assist, which still covers impact-free rollovers the authored detector never arms on. | `mm2_game::stuck` (`VehicleStuck`, `StuckEvent`), `mm2_app::stuck`, `docs/research/damage.md` |
| DSN-21 | Breakaway detachment (F05-B.3) now implements the recovered rule (REC-2): an impact whose **delivered impulse** reaches a manager-wide `10000` kg·m/s detaches exactly **one** part — the attached part whose anchor is nearest the contact point — never every part, and never on the part's authored `ImpulseLimit2` (that field only gates the *spawned fragment's* later collisions through the ordinary banger gate, OPR-1.3). The impulse is the same estimate `apply_impact_damage` accumulates and `VehicleDamage` gates on `ImpactThreshold`: approach speed × the other participant's mass, the struck car's own mass standing in for a wall hit (`mm2_app::contracts::delivered_impulse`); REC-3 measures the original's impact-record impulse as a solved contact impulse in kg·m/s on the same scale as `MaxDamage`/`ImpactThreshold`/10000, so the comparison is in kind — the estimate is a *lower bound* for the same wall hit (no `(1+e)`, full not effective mass) and an overestimate car-on-car, so `10000` is applied as a disclosed unit match, not a per-car calibration (UNK-13 keeps the magnitude open). The authored `ImpactThreshold` (1500, or 100 on `vpcaddie`) sits below the gate, so the recovered leaf's `impulse > ImpactThreshold` precondition is already satisfied when the gate trips. `VehicleBreaks::nearest_attached` scans attached parts' render-node anchors for the smallest squared distance to the contact point; a part with no node is never chosen and the rig's authored mask stays the replication truth (F25-B). On detach the intact part node hides and a fragment body spawns at the detached mesh's pose: convex hull over the part's own baked verts, the record's physicals (mass/friction/elasticity), the car's velocity at the part centroid plus the impact kick and spin, pooled through the shared `BangerPool` like prop debris. One bounded `PartDetached` fires per part per attachment; a part leaves the rig even when the pool bound denies a body (`fragment: None` on the event). The record's `CG`/`Size` conventions on vehicle fragments mix car-space anchors with ~zero conventions (not reconciled — UNK-13), so the fragment's centre of mass is the hull's measured centroid, not the record field. Repair — the disabled outcome's reset, wherever it lands — re-attaches every part and despawns its fragments; a plain reset or stuck recovery is not a repair and leaves parts off. Parts whose record is missing or malformed never enter the rig (same authored-absence rule as damage/stuck); remote participants' rigs belong to their authority (F25+). The recovered `1.25×` per-other-body re-offer gate on the impact record is not modelled — the upstream `ImpactDedup` pair window already suppresses a sustained push (REC-3). | `mm2_game::breakaway` (`VehicleBreaks`, `PartDetached`), `mm2_app::breakaway`, `docs/research/damage.md` |
| DSN-22 | Imported cars apply the recovered `vehgyro::Update` yaw torques (Drift, Spin180/Reverse180 proportional to steering and driven shaft speed) and braking-gated airborne righting, per PHY-10. The former latched yaw-rate floor/drift-relief policy remains only on the generic model. Stock Pitch/Roll values are zero or absent, so no airborne levelling is added. | `mm2_vehicle::original_step`; docs/research/vehicle-physics/04-chassis-and-assists.md; docs/vehicle-handling.md |

| DSN-23 | Water/out-of-bounds recovery (F05-B.5): no authored record exists for water or out-of-bounds rescue — the damage-family census (DMG-5..8) covers `vehcardamage`/`vehstuck`/`vehgyro` only and DMG-2 covers destruction, so every bound is designed (the original's rules stay UNK-13). `VehicleRecovery` rides on every local/AI participant (a predicted client's authority owns its own, F05 req 6), pre-anchored at spawn. Any grounded wheel under `RecoveryPolicy::water_min_drag` (0.3 — parts retail `deepwater` 0.5 from shallow `water` 0.119, which stays wadable under the F06-B.2 policy) re-anchors the last recoverable pose — but only while the car stands on what it touches: upright within 30° and, within 3 m straight below its centre of mass, dry ground belonging to no moving body (a car buried in a bank, riding a ferry deck or drawbridge leaf, or tipped up a bank keeps its old anchor — a buried pose once looped the recovery under Tower Bridge's southern junction); every grounded wheel at/above it accrues `submerge_dwell` (2.0 s — the escape window: a car that powers back onto a dry edge keeps driving) and fires a `RecoveryCause::Submerged` recovery to the anchor — back on shore, not in place on water. An airborne fall `fall_margin` (50 m) below the anchor *and* `floor_margin` (10 m) under the city's `WorldFloor` (the PSDL bounding-box minimum) fires `RecoveryCause::OutOfBounds`, latched once per fall — the margin alone is not enough because a legitimate hill jump can drop past it and a mid-air reset snatches the car back to its take-off point just before the landing; with no known floor (dev world) the margin stands alone; a non-finite pose fires at once (defence-in-depth — a pose gone NaN inside the physics step trips the wheel raycast first). `resolve_recovery` answers with `ResetVehicle` (`Teleported`, so no checkpoint sweep) to the anchor set down level on the static ground under the car's footprint (probes from the anchor's ground + footprint reach × tan 30° + 0.5 m, capped under anything overhead), falling back to the session spawn when no ground lies under the landing, trailers re-seat at authored offsets, an armed stuck episode disarms, and recovery is *not* a repair — damage and detached parts persist. Remote participants skip; `Disabled` wrecks belong to the damage outcome. `rcv=` smoke field on activity only. F28-AC04 audit: the rescue can never manufacture event progress — the reset is `Teleported`, so the swept segment breaks and the hop home clears no checkpoint, finish trigger, lap or `Ordered` gate (pinned for `AnyOrder`, `AnyOrder` + finish trigger and a two-lap `Ordered` circuit, on the drowning and the out-of-bounds leg), gates already driven stay cleared, and the retail leg audits every ready event's start slots, gates, opponent route anchors and Crash Course legs/lead cars/police posts against the real `CityWater`. What the original does when a car leaves the world mid-event (rescue, forfeit, time penalty) stays unknown (UNK-13). | `mm2_game::recovery` (`VehicleRecovery`, `RecoveryEvent`), `mm2_app::recovery`, `docs/research/damage.md` |
| DSN-24 | Engine smoke visual tier (F05-B.6): the `vehCarDamage` embedded particle spec (mm2hook's recovered `EngineSmokeRule`) renders as billboard puffs. Authored: both pivots (`SmokeOffset`, non-zero `SmokeOffset2`), the `DoublePivot` gate (emit-all vs `m_CurrentPivot` alternation), every consumed particle field (`PositionVar`/`Velocity`/`Life`/`Radius`/`Drag`/`DRadius`/`DAlpha`/`Gravity`/`TexFrame` range/`Color`), and the `fxpt` atlas-tile vocabulary (`fxpt8` is a measured 2×2 smoke atlas — white, white, black, grey — and frames index tiles like `asSparkPos::TexCoordOffset`; 17 of 20 records author tile 2, the black puff, under a white `Color`; `fxpt2`, an earlier guess, is a sheet of coins). Designed (`vehCarDamage::Update()` is a binary thunk, UNK-13): the MedDamage→MaxDamage emission ramp (4→24 puffs/s), the mirror-about-x reading of a non-zero `MirrorPivot` (all retail values are 0), the per-field integrator readings (gravity as +Y rise, exponential drag, byte-space `DAlpha` fade), the `fxpt8` texture binding (inferred: no record names a texture), and the 48-puff live bound. | DSN-45 | Route-bound AI progress (F14-A continuation): retail `.opp` lines themselves miss gate cylinders — on four cataloged Circuit events (london `circuit:2`/`4`/`5`, sf `circuit:7`; misses ~11–120 m, the F14-A.2 matrix) and on 221 of the 335 checkpoint-race lines — and a trigger-only binding stalls an opponent at the first gate its line misses. Ours keeps gate-by-gate accounting (standings and the HUD read it) but binds it to the driven line: each opponent with a route carries a `RouteGateLine` bound at spawn from its guide (`RouteGateLine::bind_arcs`) — every gate at the arc where the guide passes closest to it in plan, re-based to the spawn arc; on a lap a gate that lies before its predecessor is counted round the lap from it (the start line closing a lap the car began just short of is a whole lap away, not already passed), on an open line it is clamped to its predecessor — and under `AnyOrder` the finish too (`bind_finish_arc`: the finish trigger's closest approach, or the end of the line when the event has none). `advance_race` credits gates as the driver's high-water arc passes them — in authored order under `Ordered`, each independently under `AnyOrder`, which finishes once every gate is cleared and the arc is within the original's 20 m (`ROUTE_FINISH_RANGE`) of the finish's. Physical swept crossings still credit first wherever the car does thread a cylinder (a triggered gate never double-counts); the local player never carries the binding and a route-less entry binds nothing. Progress is what was driven: the high-water arc is the car's start plus the net metres of guide it has driven (`NeuralState::progress`), a car's place on its guide moves toward where the car is by at most three times the distance the car moved that step and not at all while it is more than 25 m off the line (`Guide::follow`, `guide::ON_LINE`), a car that was moved rather than drove is found again only *behind* where it was (`NeuralState::reseat`), and a lap adds the guide's length — so a punt, a recovery or a re-anchor banks nothing. Route-derived clears count on `RaceProgress::route_clears` separate from physical `crossings` and surface in the smoke `opps=` row as `/Nd` on activity only. | DSN-66 | Scripted racing line (F15): designed. **Since 2026-10-14 this controller drives only the scripted evidence player (`--bot`), and the police chase shares its steering; race opponents are driven by the learned policy of DSN-112**, and the opponent-only parts described below — the wall feelers, the rear-end guard, the pass corridor and the authored `cornerSpeedMultiplier`/`cornerBrakingThreshold` consumption — were removed with the scripted opponent law. The original controller is read out of the executable (docs/research/opponent-ai.md) and works differently — it drives road by road along a planned 40-point path with arcs through the bends, steers proportionally at that path, and brakes from a fixed grip budget with `cornerSpeedMultiplier` scaling corner *speed* around 1.0 — so what follows is our own policy, not a reading of the original. Pure-pursuit aim: the driver projects itself onto the leg into its chase index (`racing_line::RouteCursor`) and steers at the point `aim_distance(speed)` further down the polyline — 8 m + 0.7 s of travel, bounded 12–40 m — instead of at the next raw anchor (retail anchors sit 40–200 m apart, so the raw chase only saw a corner inside the 14 m reach radius, and a car shoved off the line aimed straight across the block at a far anchor). An open route still approaching its first anchor keeps the anchor as its aim. Planned pace: `racing_line::plan_speed` samples the line every 4 m out to braking distance (+40 m, ≤320 m), reads each stretch's radius from its heading change across a ±12 m window, and caps the speed the car may carry now at the speed from which it can still brake to every corner's grip speed (≥7 m/s). The car's own handling sets the numbers (`CarLimits::of`): corner grip is 0.7 of `HandlingMetrics`' peak lateral g (or the tip threshold if lower) scaled by the authored `cornerSpeedMultiplier / 2.0` (clamped 0.5–1.2), braking 0.6 of the brakes' or tires' straight-line limit. `racing_line::pace` drives at the throttle ceiling under the cap, holds inside a 0.5 m/s deadband, and over it consumes `cornerBrakingThreshold` as R3's brake-demand floor: a corner needing less than that share of full braking is made by lifting off, one needing more by braking at its demand (threshold capped at 0.9). The bearing law's fixed 16 m/s corner brake and 30 m/s cap no longer apply to opponents. Steering (`racing_line::steer_toward`) is pure pursuit from the car's own geometry: the arc through the aim point (curvature `2·sin(bearing)/distance`) becomes a wheel angle through the wheelbase, plus the front tires' peak slip angle scaled by the share of their grip the arc uses at the current speed, divided by the car's speed-interpolated steering lock and passed through the inverse of its input shaping; an aim behind the car takes full lock toward it. The chase index, reach radius, re-anchor and route-bound progress are unchanged. Wall feelers (`racing_line::sense_walls`): each frame four horizontal rays from 0.5 m above the body origin — ±0.3 rad reaching 6 m + 0.6 s of travel (≤30 m) and ±0.8 rad reaching 3 m + 0.15 s — against static bodies only (other cars and dynamic props are the corridor's and the recovery law's); a hit whose normal points more than 0.6 up is ground, not wall. Each side's closeness is the worst `(1 − hit/len)²`, and the steering gains `0.8 × (left − right)` away from the closer wall. A freshly armed reverse-and-turn escape turns toward the side the feelers see as open instead of alternating blind. Rear-end guard: a fellow opponent the authored `avoidOpponents=0` leaves unsensed is still looked for straight ahead (the corridor's ±2.4 m), out to the driver's stopping distance at the planned deceleration plus 20 m (capped at its look-ahead). The guard commits no pass — the flag keeps deciding whether the driver manoeuvres around the field — and only answers a closure: the demand `closing² / (2 × (gap − 5 m))` over the car's full braking lifts off from 0.15 and brakes at the demand from 0.3, and a standing car approached under 2 m/s is left to the escape and the feelers so the guard cannot pen the driver behind it. The human participant is not covered — `avoidPlayers=0` drivers still ram the player. Measured on the 16-event parked-Pro benchmark: hard (≥5 m/s) AI-on-AI impacts 203 → 112 while the total contact count stayed level, the hits replaced by low-speed nudges. Authored routes with local samples closer than the legacy 14 m anchor reach use spacing-bounded reach plus adjacent-leg physical projection, retaining upcoming bends for the speed planner; widely spaced routes preserve the original 14 m reach/plane heuristic. This designed sampling correction does not alter authored speed demands, physics or race credit. Guided evidence-player steering also reuses this planner: speed-scaled projected lookahead is capped at the live player checkpoint, handling-derived steering and curvature pace issue ordinary inputs, and the existing bounded scripted recovery is retained. This does not give the player route-bound AI checkpoint credit, catch-up assistance or authored opponent tuning. Explicit evidence guides additionally retain short pursuit through discrete sharp-junction tangents, with entry braking and a corner speed derived from the existing car steering lock and grip; straight and smoothly sampled bends keep the normal planner. This designed narrow-junction correction changes player inputs only, preserving native opponent policy, checkpoint authority, timers and vehicle physics. | `mm2_app::racing_line` (`RouteCursor`, `CarLimits`, `plan_speed`, `pace`, `steer_toward`), `mm2_app::opponents` (`aim_distance`), `mm2_app::scripted` (`planned_route_aim`, `planned_input`), `mm2_app::police` (`chase_input`) |

The race clock keeps running; the timer freezes outside `Playing`; AI/remote wrecks keep their instant in-place repair; Crash Course keeps `RestartEvent`; Circuit keeps its DSN time-penalty reset. | DSN-70 | Networked world props (F26-A): designed. The host owns every banger transition; a joined client's copy of the world follows it as replicated state. Each stamped banger placement carries a `BangerSite` — its ordinal in stamp order, the same on every process loading the same content — and the host's `Message::Props` frames carry every active body, every prop that just changed and a rolling window of settled/broken ones as `(site, fragment, phase, pose)` rows; the client makes an active prop a kinematic body at the wire pose, a settled one a static collider at its resting pose, shatters a broken placement and spawns its pieces from its own authored `BREAK<NN>` chunks. Not an original-behaviour claim — how the original's DirectPlay sessions synced props is not researched here, and original-client compatibility is out of scope; the policy is ours. Poses are not interpolated and no velocity rides the wire. Each frame also carries the host's `SiteTable` (placement count + digest of ordinal/name/home); a client whose own stamped world differs refuses the rows rather than misattribute shifted ordinals (v18). A late joiner is caught up by a paced snapshot of every non-default site (F26-A.3: every placement that has left its authored pose, `SNAPSHOT_PACE` 32 rows a frame, superseding the rolling window until delivered; only a frame whose send left advances it) rather than waiting a resend cycle — a host pacing policy over the same rows, no wire change and no original-protocol claim. | `mm2_game::banger` (`BangerSite`, `BangerFragment`), `mm2_app::worldprops`, `mm2_net::proto` (`Message::Props`), `docs/research/net.md` |

MP-4 documents no ambient traffic, cops or AI opponents in multiplayer *races*, and names no Cruise exception; the F26 spec asks Cruise clients to share traffic. | DSN-76 | Driving control settings (F23-A.1): designed (enhanced policy) over CTL-1/CTL-3. `mm2_app::controls::ControlSettings` is the one device-mapping table — up to two keys for each of five driving actions (accelerate, brake/reverse, steer left/right, handbrake), plus the pad's stick/trigger deadzones (0–0.9), steering sensitivity (0.25–2.0) and stick inversion — and `drive_input` normalizes keys + pad into `VehicleInput`, so physics never sees a key code. `Default` is the shipped map (W/↑, S/↓, A/←, D/→, Space; 0.10 stick / 0.05 trigger deadzone; gain 1), not the original's arrows-only CTL-1 layout. CTL-3's "conflict warning" is read as *refuse and name the owner* (`BindError::Conflict`) rather than silently stealing the key; an action always keeps ≥1 key; keys limited to letters/digits/arrows/Space/Shift and never one the in-session controls own (`RESERVED_KEYS`). Persisted machine-wide to `controls.json` beside `settings.json` (atomic write, shared writer); loading repairs each invalid piece to its default and warns, rejects a key two actions claim as a whole, and an unreadable/unparseable file yields the defaults; evidence runs neither read nor write it. The stick's deadzone is removed and the remaining travel rescaled to 0…1 (saturating at 0.95 deflection), and the stick then runs through retail's joystick shaper (`mmPlayer::FilterSteering`, 05-player-input.md; per-car `Joy*` tune tokens, `JoystickSteering::COMMON` where a car has none — designed stand-in, the constructor defaults are untranscribed) instead of the keyboard ramp: the stick is divided by the sensitivity (2.5 on every authored car) so a small tilt stays small at any speed, and only a stick held at its stop ramps toward full lock. The keyboard keeps the recovered ramp (retail files a gamepad under the keyboard path; using the joystick path for a pad's stick is designed). Under `Manual`, `input::vehicle_input` pins the gearbox through `VehicleInput::forced_gear` from a `ManualGear` hold: seeded from the car's current gear (switching never lurches), one press of a shift key (`DriveAction::ShiftUp`/`ShiftDown`, default `G`/`B`, rebindable like the driving keys) is one gear, clamped to the car's authored gear count, the hold is dropped on any non-driving frame and re-seeded on a new car. The documented `A`/`Z` are not the defaults because `A` steers and `Z` is the nav-arrow key in the shipped map. Pad shift buttons are not rebindable (pad rebinding is open). The wire carries no gear (`forced_gear` never rides `DriveInput`), so the policy is inert on a predicted (`Remote`) session — the host's copy stays automatic and a locally pinned prediction would diverge. Reverse is unchanged (brake once stopped). Original `T`/`A`/`Z` timing, whether the original's manual box auto-downshifts, and its rev-limit behaviour are unverified. | `mm2_app::controls`, `mm2_app::manual_gear`, `mm2_app::input::vehicle_input`, `tests/input.rs` |

One-shots are scaled the frame they spawn by `audio::level_new_voices` (PostUpdate, before transform propagation, so a sink is never created at the authored volume), looping mixers (`engine_drive`, `ambient_engine_drive`, weather beds, skid/rolling, object sounds) multiply the same gain into every `set_volume`; an in-flight one-shot keeps the level it spawned at. Rows are on the main menu's Options screen and the pause overlay's graphics page. | DSN-79 | Lobby rematch (F26-B.1): designed. MP-8 documents the lobby's Ready/Start Server controls only; whether the retail game returns to the lobby for another round is unverified and no original rematch rule is claimed. Policy: after a session ends the same lobby, roster and sockets carry the next round (`Cancel` clears readiness, keeps picks); a client launched `--ready` stays ready across rounds so unattended clients can rematch, others re-ready by hand. A wire-driven session begin discards the previous session's queued quit/restart/pause so the new generation cannot be cancelled on arrival. Evidence: synthetic + a three-process loopback leg; no windowed or LAN run. A joiner arriving after a cruise `Start` is handed the running generation (F26-B.2 process leg, loopback, dev world); races stay closed to late joiners (MP-5): a started event lobby answers a joiner `SessionStarted` (refusal, not spectating — the original documents no spectator role, so none is designed), proven with `mm2-host --event` in `net_host.rs`. A client *process* (`mm2-join`) that accepted round one and hears a re-advertised session its mount cannot run reports `session_refused` and leaves with a clean `quit` (exit 1), `net_join.rs`. A joiner after a leaver is a fresh connection with a new wire id; the leaver's seat does not return and no identity carries over (F26-B.3 process leg, loopback, dev world). The in-app host can re-advertise a *changed* session (city/mode/difficulty/conditions) for the next round between rounds, never mid-round; the late-join policy follows the new mode (F26-B.4, in-process, fixture city; the host operator types a `session key=value …` stdin word to do it, F26-B.5, with a two-process dev-world loopback leg; no windowed host menu yet). |

Radius, annulus, spacing, cap and density mapping are designed bounds, not original constants. At runtime (F19-B.2, `mm2_app::crowd`) the density is the player's pick, else the event's authored `Peds` (Circuit authors 0, CIR-3), else the session default; walkers recycle outside every player's 90 m bubble and refill two per tick; each carries a kinematic body capsule (F19-B.8b, DSN-89); reactions to cars are DSN-89. | designed | `mm2_game::pedwalk`, `mm2_app::crowd`; `mm2-inspect nav` sidewalk census (8 m: 1551/2160 London and 1222/1516 SF ends joined, 224/30 joins severed by a vehicle lane) |
| DSN-89 | Pedestrian reaction to cars (F19-B.3): `mm2_game::pedreact` + `mm2_app::crowd::react_pedestrians`. The *vocabulary* is authored (every stock `pedmodel_*.csv` carries `ANTIC` with its `WALK_ANTIC`/`ANTIC_WALK` transitions, `ANTIC_/WALK_` `LDIVE`/`RDIVE` and the ground-recovery chain ending in `STAND`; `mm2-inspect peds` shows each dive chain reaching `STAND` on all four archetypes, 3.1–3.6 s long and 4.38 m (man/manw) or 4.99 m (woman/womanw) across, lateral travel in the csv `X AXIS` columns, left positive); the *rules* are the recovered sensing (UNK-43, F19-B.7) with designed timers. A walker senses only **player-driven** cars (`Player` without `PlayerControl::Ai` — retail's four player slots) within 35 m (retail) and faster than 1 m/s (retail), keeping the nearest by horizontal distance, taken to keep its velocity. It is in the near corridor (retail `0x54e660`) when it is past 0.25 of the car's length and within 20 m along the car's axis and half the car's width + 2 m to the side; there a time value `(L − 2)/speed` seconds (retail; `L` is the along-axis distance) < 0.75 s (retail) dives it and < 2.3 s (retail) stops it. Outside that it is in the far corridor (retail `0x54e8a0`, to 35 m along the axis and half-width + 4 m to the side) which only stops it. A car steering `> 0.85` sends the dive right and `< −0.85` sends it left (retail `0x54dc3f`, both strict); below that the walker dives to its own side of the car's line (`ANTIC_xDIVE` when watching, `WALK_xDIVE` mid-stride). It travels the csv's lateral distance linearly through each row and holds it through the ground rows; at `STAND` it walks back to its sidewalk curve at walking speed and carries on. A wary walker walks on after 1 s without a threat or 8 s regardless; a dive not at `STAND` after 6 s rejoins anyway. The walker's *body* is a kinematic torso capsule derived from its bind-pose skin bounds (radius half the fore-aft depth, bounded 0.2–0.35 m — the T-pose's arm span is cosmetic) on a `Pedestrian` collision layer that meets the default layer the cars ride on and nothing else (F19-B.8b): the authored walk/dive still drives the `Transform`, Avian carries the body with it at zero velocity, so a car that catches a walker is blocked at the body (bounded, no interpenetration) and the walker is never moved by the car — it plays only its authored stop/look/dive chain, so this stays a reaction model with no run-over, struck state, damage, score or sound (F19 req 4). Wheel, suspension, camera and recovery rays skip the `Pedestrian` layer (#1390; only the chassis pair and the contact-point re-cast see a walker, `pedanim.md` "Which spatial queries see a walker"). An archetype lacking any state in `REACTION_STATES` walks on obliviously. The measured car-side response (`0x55b154`: car field `+0x153c` = 2.0 vs 1.0) is recorded but not remapped — vehicle feel is owner-owned. | designed | `mm2_game::pedreact` (`ReactPolicy`, `assess`, `Reaction`, `dive_lateral`), `mm2_app::crowd` (`react_pedestrians`), `mm2_app::pedestrian` (`body_collider`, the kinematic body in `spawn_pedestrian`), `mm2_app::layers` (`GameLayer::Pedestrian`), `mm2-inspect peds` reaction-chain leg; tests `mm2_app` `tests/crowd.rs` (`a_car_catching_a_walker_contacts_its_body_and_its_authored_avoidance_still_runs`, `a_car_bearing_down_makes_a_walker_look_dive_clear_and_walk_back`) |
| DSN-90 | Pedestrians in networked sessions (F19-B.5): the sidewalk crowd is cosmetic (no score, no rule reads it; each process's own walkers carry a local kinematic body capsule, F19-B.8b, but it is not replicated), so nobody is its authority and it is not replicated. The host and every `Remote` client run the same `maintain`/`react`/`walk` systems from the same resolved density and seed, around the players that process holds (a client's kinematic copies of remote cars included, whose replicated velocity is what a walker senses). Two processes therefore see different walkers on the same kerb; a pedestrian in a networked Cruise is a local presentation, unlike ambient traffic (DSN-71), which cars can hit and so is host-published. Whether the original's multiplayer cities carried pedestrians is unverified — the policy is ours. A `Remote` client cannot pause (MP-6), so its crowd never freezes on a local pause. | designed | `mm2_app::crowd`, `mm2_app` `tests/crowd.rs` (`a_remote_client_*`) |
| DSN-91 | Mod impact classification (F29 req 5): a mounted mod is *cosmetic-only* when it wins no logical path `mm2_content::fingerprint::is_gameplay_path` classes as gameplay (tuning, bounds, geometry, race/anim/player records, city data except sky/lighting/visibility), judged by the files it actually wins (`mod_reports`). Gameplay mods move the multiplayer fingerprint and make the run record-ineligible (`Ineligible::ModContent`); cosmetic-only mods do neither, so a repaint or sound swap keeps records and unlocks. An unclassified modded session (`SessionConfig::mods_cosmetic_only` false) fails safe as ineligible. Path-based, not a byte diff, so an identical-bytes rewrite of a gameplay file still counts as gameplay. The runtime speaks the `PRERACE` line first in the lesson's pre-race window (ahead of the city announcer's weather/time-of-day lines, which are unchanged), then the `RESULTSWIN` line when the lesson is passed or the `RESULTSPOOR` line when an attempt fails, once per attempt; the triggers are the same designed reading DSN-86/87 take of the section names, not recovered behaviour. Tables are followed verbatim: London row 1 speaks `CCL03` and row 2 `CCL02` (lesson numbers look swapped against the row tags), SF row 7 (the second exam) authors `CCSFIRSTEXAMFAIL` as its failure line (it reuses the first exam's failure waves), and SF row 11 authors `CCS03THIRDEXAM*` stems. 3 of the 256 drawable waves name a file the install does not ship (london row 9 `ccl08fail05`, sf row 6 `ccs06fail02`, sf row 8 `ccs07fail03`: those families' `end` equals their file count, not their top suffix), counted as a missed cue, never re-drawn (F18-AC06). **Per-gate call-outs bound 2026-10-14 (F21-B, #1227) — this part recovered from the executable, not designed:** the school-table loader reads `cc_cpoint_waveinfo.csv` + `cc_cpoint_indexinfo.csv` only for `row == 4` (`0x51b736`), and the gate-cleared routine `0x4360b0` maps the passed course row through the index list (`-1,15,3,6,5,10,12,13`) to a suffix of the `CHECKPOINT` cue's wave list (`add + 1 + indexinfo[row]`, `0x513e00`/`0x513fd0`), speaking it on the clearing edge. The runtime speaks one line, from the speaker the pre-race cues drew, once the finish is the only gate left to cross (`Ordered`: the final lap's closing gate, which is the lifted start line; `AnyOrder` with a separate finish trigger: every checkpoint cleared, finish still armed; `AnyOrder` without one: the last uncleared gate). Only while the race is `Running`, queued behind any pre-race speech and bound by the event table kind (`mm2_game::event_speech_table`: blitz/checkpoint/circuit; cruise, Crash Course and C&R bind none). It is deliberately not tied to the `lastwaypoint` effect: under `AnyOrder` with no finish trigger that edge coincides with finishing, after commentary has stopped. A miss (no speaker, absent section/wave) counts one `failed`, never substituted. Other sections (`PRERACE`, `UNLOCK*`, `DAMAGEPENALTY`, `finallap.csv`) stay unbound (`RESULTS*` — DSN-87); the original's actual trigger and cadence are unrecovered (UNK-25). |

The names say nothing about the standing that selects a tier. The runtime speaks one line at the local participant's end of race, from the speaker the pre-race cues drew: first place on the ledger's standings (a lone racer who finishes included) reads `RESULTSWIN`; last place of a field of two or more reads `RESULTSPOOR`; between reads `RESULTSMID`, falling to `RESULTSPOOR` for a table that authors no middle tier (blitz); a time-out reads `RESULTSPOOR`. A finish with no ranked place (no ledger) says nothing. Places are among recorded finishers only (the ledger's designed ordering), so a player who finishes before the field is first. The queue drains in `Results` as well as the live window, so the line plays after the finish; a session that reaches Results without ever running the pre-race window drew no announcer and counts one `failed`. Never substituted otherwise (F18-AC06). `UNLOCK*` (the next-event unlock) stays unbound; the original's tier rule and cadence are unrecovered (UNK-25). |

| DSN-112 | Neural opponent driver (F15): designed, and a deliberate departure — the original's controller is known (UNK-11) and is not what drives our opponents. A small `tanh` MLP (132 inputs, two hidden layers of 256, 3 outputs; ~100k weights, evaluated on the CPU) trained by reinforcement learning in this engine's own vehicle simulation turns what a car can see into steering, a pedal axis (throttle positive, brake negative, capped at the row's authored `maxThrottle`) and the handbrake, deciding at 20 Hz — every third physics step — and holding its action between. It sees, in the car's own frame: what car it is (size, mass, power, top speed, steering lock, grip, throttle ceiling); its motion; its *guide* — offset and heading against the line and the line at twelve distances from 5 m to 340 m (braking distance from the fastest cars' 77 m/s); 24 world rays (three down the corridor the car is about to sweep, then a fan; carried along ground they meet, blind to vehicles, deadly water read as open space); six floor probes that find a drop ahead; and the four nearest other vehicles of any kind — players, opponents, police, ambient traffic. It is given no race state: nobody's progress, no positions in the field, no rubber band (DSN-27 withdrawn). Route choice stays authored (the driving line of DSN-114), progress and finishing stay rules (DSN-45), and a stalled car is backed out by rule (DSN-113). One policy per difficulty is compiled into the binary (`crates/mm2_app/policies/`); a mod replaces either at `ai/opponent_{amateur,professional}.mm2p`, and a file trained for another observation layout is refused, never driven on misaligned inputs. The guide is the whole interface to *where*: a nav-graph path or a straight segment to a target drives the same way, which is what a Cops & Robbers car or a pursuer would hand it (training includes road-graph routes with no authored line). Training pipeline, evaluation and measured results: `docs/neural-opponents.md`. | `mm2_app::neural` (`observe`, `policy`, `drive`, `guide`, `env`), `mm2_app::opponents` (`opponent_drive`), `training/` |
| DSN-113 | Opponent back-up (F15-B): an opponent that has held more than 0.3 of the pedal for 30 decisions (1.5 s) without moving a metre is taken over for at most 22 decisions (1.1 s) and reversed — full reverse, steering `−2.857 ×` the bearing to its line 15 m on, ending early once the nose is within 0.1 rad of it — then handed back to its policy. Two departures from the original's manoeuvre (2026-10-14): every back-up lasts at least 10 decisions (0.5 s) before alignment may end it — a car nose-on to something *on* its line is aligned already, and was handed back before it had moved — and a back-up that starts within 6 m of the last one reverses for 30 decisions on full lock instead, one side and then the other, so the policy meets the obstacle from a new angle. The manoeuvre and its constants are the original's `aiVehiclePhysics` state 1 (reverse for 66 frames at that steering gain, ending inside 0.1 rad; UNK-11); the trigger is designed (the original arms on a collision and its own `vehStuck` thresholds) and none of the original's direct edits to the rigid body (momentum zeroed on entry, × 0.25 and an orientation snap on exit) are made. It exists because a driver rewarded for progress almost never discovers reversing; the same code runs in training, so the policy learns from the states a back-up leaves it in. Counted per car in the smoke `opps=` row as `/Ne`. | `mm2_app::neural::unstick`, `mm2_app::neural::drive` (`NeuralState::drive`), `mm2_app::neural::env` |
| DSN-114 | Opponent driving line (F15-B.6): the reading of an `.opp` record is the original's (UNK-11, verified) — row 0 the start pose, on which the opponent stages (UNK-17); rows 1…n−2 the course's intersections, consecutive ones adjacent; the last row the finish position; a circuit wrapping from the end of the list to row 1. So a circuit's lap is its intersection rows alone (`opponents::circuit_lap`; closing the loop through the start pose and the finish position, which stand *on* the lap, sent every car back past its own grid twice a lap; a record is a circuit's when its staging row and its finish position both stand on the road out of the first intersection or the one back into it — not when the finish is near the staging row, which fails the back of a grid: `sf circuit2`, `sf circuit6` and `london circuit3` each had cars staged 40 m and more behind the finish, read as open routes one lap long, that stopped on the finish position with laps to go; and a finish written twice, as `london circuit3`'s VW Cup rows give it after stopping two intersections short of the lap, is one finish), and a leg between two rows that name adjacent intersections runs along the one road they share whichever way its traffic flows (`NavGraph::race_leg`, the original's `aiMap::FindRoadBetween`) — the directed router, asked for a turn against the flow, went round the block or found no route and left the leg a straight line through the buildings. What is designed is the line laid on that reading: down the middle of the lanes that run the leg's way (a divided road's centre line is a median), or of whatever lanes the road has when driven against its flow; for a leg whose rows name two intersections with no road between them — retail records skip one here and there: `sf circuit8` names the two ends of the hill loop east of Alta Plaza and not the junction between, both roads one-way against the lap, and the directed router turned the field round at the first to drive back the way it came — the chain of roads that joins them, flow ignored, preferring roads with one of the race's checkpoints on them (a tenth the cost per metre) and never opening back toward the row the car came from (`NavGraph::race_path`: ≤ 6 roads, ≤ 2.5× the straight line unless a checkpoint explains the detour; what the original does with such a leg is not known — `FindRoadBetween` finds no road); the directed router and then the authored straight line as the last fall-backs; no checkpoint *constraining* the re-path (gates are earned by route position, DSN-45); and, for the driver's guide, heights taken every 10 m from the road lane under the line (`Guide::from_route_on` — the straight line between two rows 200 m apart runs up to 12 m under a San Francisco crest) a ±10 m smoothing that takes out the few-metre jink where an authored point inside a junction meets the road lines either side, and — once the collision world exists, before the start — a pass of rays that moves any point more than 1 m from the ground under it onto that ground (`Guide::regrounded`: a course does not keep to roads, and across a park or down a terrace the line between two rows stood up to 11 m off the ground; heights only, arc positions and everything bound to them unchanged), and a second that moves the line up to 4.5 m sideways off anything within a car's width of it that a car could not push aside — the static world, and props of 500 kg and more: parked cars, street lights, trees — where there is level ground to move it to (`Guide::cleared`). | DSN-117 | Race-start hold for AI opponents (#1445): a countdown holds every car's input, but a held opponent previously wrote a neutral pedal (handbrake 0), so on a sloped starting grid gravity rolled it off its authored row-0 pose (UNK-17) and into GO with residual backward momentum. The original's stopped-hold — handbrake forced on below 4 mph off the throttle (PHY-12, `mmPlayer::Update`) — is armed only for the human driver; the start grid itself holds any car through the not-drivable override (`vehCar::PreUpdate` mode 1: brake on, neutral gear, throttle and steering live — 04-chassis-and-assists.md), which this port does not model. The port's countdown instead holds the human by zeroing its input and arming the stopped-hold, so the reading here is designed: while `RaceState::input_locked()` holds the field, an opponent that is not driving takes that same standing handbrake, keeping its grid pose and carrying no linear or angular momentum into GO. Released, the car drives normally with no launch impulse. Measured on retail: all 12 San Francisco Checkpoint grids hold (drift < 0.75 m, < 0.1 m/s) where the unfixed build slid one 6.2 m down the hill; a 6° synthetic grid is the deterministic leg. | `mm2_app::opponents` (`opponent_drive`); tests `mm2_app` `opponents::a_sloped_grid_holds_opponents_through_the_countdown`, `opponents::opponents_release_from_a_sloped_grid_without_backward_momentum`, `opponents_retail::retail_sloped_grids_hold_opponents_through_the_countdown` |
| DSN-118 | World-simulation readiness gate (#1446): `load_session_world` builds the whole session world inside one `Update`, so the time that frame took (how long the map took to load) becomes the next frame's `Time<Real>` delta, which Bevy clamps to `Time<Virtual>::max_delta` and replays as up to fifteen catch-up fixed steps the moment the world exists — so a slower load handed opponents and traffic extra simulation, and a head start to the countdown, before the race began. Designed barrier: `mm2_app::readiness::gate_world_simulation` runs in `First` ahead of `TimeSystems` and, on the first `Countdown`/`Playing` frame of each session generation (exactly the frame the load's wall time lands on), caps `Time<Virtual>` at one fixed timestep — dropping the load's time instead of replaying it — then restores the clock on the following frame. A steady frame (`delta == timestep`) is unaffected, every phase-gated system already held outside `Countdown`/`Playing`/`Results`, and traffic is covered by the same fixed-loop barrier. Evidence is the deterministic regression pair: with the gate a 400 ms load frame runs exactly one fixed step and the start state (countdown, simulation clock, every opponent pose/velocity) is identical to a one-frame load; without it the slow run loses 13 countdown ticks and rolls the field down the grid. Designed, not a reading of retail — the original is a different engine and its load barrier is not recovered. | `mm2_app::readiness` (`gate_world_simulation`, `SimulationGate`); wired in `mm2_app::{main,smoke}`; tests `mm2_app` `readiness::a_loads_wall_time_is_dropped_not_replayed`, `opponents::a_slow_load_starts_the_race_like_a_fast_one` |
| DSN-119 | No trailer reverse/brake lamp, and therefore no trailer reverse state on the wire (F25-AC05). A trailer's authored lighting is a single `TLIGHT_L` quad: `mm2-inspect pkg <install> geometry/{vpsemi,vpcentury,vpftruck,vplafrance}_trailer.pkg` shows every one of the four shipped trailers carries `TLIGHT_L` (the `fxltglowred` taillight shader, painted `vptrailer_{red,yellow,green}_backlight`) and no `RLIGHT` (reverse) or `BLIGHT` (brake) chunk at all, while the tow car authors the full lamp set (`vpsemi.pkg`: `HLIGHT_L`, `TLIGHT_L`, `RLIGHT_L`). The trailers also have no reverse gear — the sim's `DriveDirection` policy keeps a trailer's brake input a brake (`mm2_vehicle::systems`, `!cfg.trailer` guards the reverse transition), so a trailer's `VehicleState::direction` never becomes `Reverse` — and `car_visual::update_glows` lights a `GlowKind::Reverse` part only for a car whose direction *is* `Reverse` and whose model authored an `rlight` part; neither holds on a trailer. There is thus no trailer reverse presentation to keep correct, so the port deliberately carries no trailer reverse state: a `SnapTrailer` row keeps only the grounded bit (`SnapTrailer::flags`), brake/handbrake ride the tractor's input through `trailer_input`, and the copy has no reverse state of its own. The reverse *motion* that does exist — a backing trailer's wheels turning the other way — is already on the wire as the signed wheel `spin` cell, counted per trailer wheel by `NetDriveReport::trailers_driven` (`trl=…,turn<k>`) and per follower wheel by `xwhl=<driven>/<total>` (#1257). This is a designed no-gap conclusion, not a false economy: the wire omits nothing the authored content could present. | verified_original (lamp census of the four `*_trailer.pkg`); designed (the no-state conclusion); `mm2-inspect pkg` on retail; `crates/mm2_vehicle/src/systems.rs`; `crates/mm2_app/src/car_visual.rs`/`netdrive.rs`; docs/research/net.md |
