| UNK-11 | Opponent AI — narrowed 2026-10-04 by reading the controller out of `Midtown2.exe` (docs/research/opponent-ai.md). **Now verified**: the roster side (RACE-11/RACE-12); the `.opp` grammar the original reads — row 0 is the start pose (position plus the mislabelled `brake` column as a heading in degrees), rows 1…n−2 must each lie in an intersection room and become a chain of *adjacent* intersections, the last row is the finish; the other columns are never read, and the original does no route choice of its own between `.opp` points (`0x53d040`, `0x53a660`); the parameter tail's semantics (RACE-14); that opponents drive the player's `.vehcarsim`, never the `_opp` files (RACE-13); the controller itself — a 40-point path along the BAI roads with racing-line arcs through precomputed bends, a recursive detour search around obstacles, proportional steering at the path, full `maxThrottle` or braking from a fixed 23.76 m/s² grip budget; that it applies **no** catch-up — the tuning is written once and nothing reads another participant's progress (contrast DSN-27); that a stuck car backs up (no teleport — contrast DSN-14) and a car fallen through the world stays out; and that the controller edits its own car's rigid-body state (yaw damping while braking, momentum cuts around the backup, an orientation snap). and how an opponent finishes: `aiVehicleOpponent` decides it itself (`0x53d6c0`) — last lap, route index at its last intersection, and a crossing of the finish line's plane within 20 m of the finish point; **checkpoints play no part**, which the retail routes rely on (221 of 335 checkpoint-race `.opp` lines miss a gate cylinder, 186 gates by more than 100 m). Ours follows it in substance: an opponent's gates and finish are bound to positions on its own driven line and earned by driving it (DSN-45), whichever rule the event runs under. **Still unverified**: what the spare `.opp` files and `race/sf/race5-a-{5,6,7}`'s mid-file staging rows were for; `unkFlag`'s reader; the exact point spacing and detour scoring of the planner; and the meaning of the per-frame `CarFrictionHandling` switch. |
| UNK-12 | Traffic/pedestrian ambient models (lanes, lights, panic reactions). `.bai` parses and builds a directed nav graph (`docs/research/bai.md`), `.aimap` overrides parse (`docs/research/aimap.md`) — how the original runtime consumes those remains unverified. `.pathset` placements parse and `city/<city>/{props,decals}.pathset` now stamp ambient prop rows (WLD-13) and decal ribbons (WLD-18); audio/race pathset consumption is still unimplemented. Runtime note (F10-A.1): the authored side of ambient traffic is now measured (WLD-20) — `.aivehicledata` tuning decodes, `[Ambient Types/Density]` cumulative rosters import per city and per event — and `mm2_game::traffic::plan_ambient` plans seeded spawns over routable lanes honouring `NavOverrides` closures. Still unverified: the original's per-room pool size, per-lane quotas, ambient flag semantics, and whether density gates count or rate. Recovered from `Midtown2.exe` (F10-SIGNAL-TIMING, `verified_original`): there is no spawn ring — the original's simulated set is the authored per-room cull bubble (manager `+0x174` large / `+0x178` small, diffed on a room change by `0x539db0`, remove via `0x547a30`/`0x547be0`, add via `0x547a90`), a new car must sit ≥ 50 m from every existing ambient body (`dat_5aff6c = 2500.0f` squared, `0x53a2f5`), and the map density is `clamp(density, 0, 1) × 0.2` (`dat_5af618`, `0x53551d`) — docs/research/bai.md. The same code also runs from a non-room-change path (`0x53b6a4`/`0x53b74f`) whose trigger is unidentified. Runtime note (F10-B.2): the BAI `vehicleRule` road-end codes are now consumed — `mm2_game::traffic::Junctions` gates each lane-end transfer by the authored approach rule: `TrafficLight` approaches wait for their road's phase in a deterministic per-junction member cycle (documented "one road at a time"; signal membership measured — docs/research/bai.md), `StopSign` approaches pass a first-come-first-served queue after a dwell (documented "longest waiting vehicle drives first"), `AlwaysStop` never opens, `NeverStop` flows. Recovered from `Midtown2.exe` (F10-SIGNAL-TIMING, `verified_original`): the light controller's phase is **6.0 s** (`0x53c04f` stores `0x40c00000` into `+0x2c`; ticked at 1/60 s, `0x53c490`), it runs a **3.0 s amber** (`dat_5af4e8`, `0x53c61c`), the **all-red clearance is one whole phase (6.0 s)** and applies to control types 1 and 2 only (`0x53c4f4`–`0x53c53f`), and there is **no stop-dwell timer** — a `StopSign` car joins the per-end first-come-first-served queue only when stopped (< 0.5 m/s, `dat_5af360`) within 1.5 m of the line (`dat_5b017c`), which `0x54a3a0` enqueues and `0x54a4c0` clears, decoded through the `vehicleRule` jump table at `0x540b54` (docs/research/bai.md). The port now adopts these (`JunctionPolicy::default`: `green_ticks` 6 s, `clear_ticks` 6 s, `stop_dwell_ticks` 0, `stop_inset` 1.5 m, #1477); it still draws no amber (presentation only — the lamp stays green through it), and the approach ramp, `stop_line_tolerance`, decel, entry clearance and box sizing remain designed/unrecovered. Ambient spawns now enforce the recovered ≥ 50 m separation from every live ambient car and earlier placement (`SpawnPolicy::min_ambient_separation`, `too_close_to_ambient`; 3D vs. ground-plane measure unrecovered); the `clamp(density,0,1)*0.2` map density is deliberately not adopted — the port's `max_active` pool and the `0.2` factor's base are unrecovered, so density × `max_active` stays designed. Runtime note (F10-B.6): ambient cars now carry authored `Mass`/CG and a kinematic→dynamic collision handover exists — a `CollisionStart` contact whose `approach_speed × striker_mass` estimate reaches the designed `KnockPolicy::min_impulse` (4000 N·s) converts the same entity to `RigidBody::Dynamic`, drops it from lane driving/FCFS, and leaves a solver-owned wreck that still occupies the junction box until the distance recycler collects it. The threshold, the linear impulse estimate (banger activation instead gates on striker kinetic energy `½·m·v²` since F04-C.5, DSN-10), the bounded contact-normal velocity kick, and the wreck-persistence policy are all designed — the original's ambient crash rules (do ambient cars ever deform, shove, or despawn on impact?) are unverified. Runtime note (F10-B.7): the authored `trafficLightOrigin`/`trafficLightAxis` road-end pairs now surface as session-owned indicators — `NavGraph::signals` carries every nonzero origin on a resolved end (828 London, 647 SF after 3 authored >60 m outliers drop on a designed sanity bound; ~47% of London's heads govern ends no vehicle arc approaches, so arc-exit-only traversal would miss them). `drive_signals` maps each head's aspect off `Junctions` — green while the end's road holds the phase, red otherwise/in all-red, constant green on free-flow or non-member ends, a stop aspect on stop-signed ends. The indicator geometry (the retail `sp_traflit*` unit on the authored origin, WLD-35; variant and facing designed), the 60 m bound, and the aspect mapping are all designed — the original's signal-unit draw is not reproduced: the geometry ships (`sp_traflitsingle_*`/`sp_traflitdual_f`, WLD-35) and the sphere is a placeholder, while which variant each head gets, the pole/arm placement and the exact lamp geometry per state remain unverified (state timing is recovered — F10-SIGNAL-TIMING); the authored positions and rules are original data, and `trafficLightAxis` is measured as a horizontal unit cross-road vector stored as `origin + axis` (docs/research/bai.md). Runtime note (F10-B.8): the ambient population now lives in the union of player interest areas — `in_spawn_band`/`within_interest` replace the single-player bubble for both the plan draw and the runtime recycler: a car is collected only past `recycle_distance` of *every* `Player` participant (local driver, remote drivers, AI opponents — designed composition: each can hold corridor/box/collision interactions with ambient cars), and a spawn must land inside at least one area's band while staying outside every area's `min_player_distance`. The original's interest-area model (per-player bubbles? AI-held areas?) is unverified; the spec's union requirement and the distance constants remain designed. Runtime note (F10-B.9): lane transfers now cross the junction interior instead of teleporting between lane extremities — `NavGraph::crossing_path` generates a cubic Hermite from the approach lane's travel-direction end to the exit lane's start, and `LaneCursor` traverses it incrementally while keeping the approach lane identity (junction occupancy/right-of-way preserved; `LaneAdvance::Entered`/`Landed` mark commit/landing). The generated geometry, its chord-scaled tangents and the resample step are designed — BAI carries no authored junction-interior curves and the original's runtime crossing path is unrecovered. The same census surfaced an authored-data quirk now handled at build: several SF lane curves are vertex-reversed relative to section order (authoring artefact, not a direction marker — `docs/research/bai.md`), so `NavGraph::build` normalizes curve order before sampling. A pose-continuity watchdog (`AmbientTraffic::jumps`, `jmp=` in the headless record) counts per-update teleport-like moves; retail headless runs report `crx=59/68 jmp=0` on sf/london. |

| UNK-22 | Banger runtime semantics (WLD-15/16). **Partly recovered (OPR-1.3, read from `Midtown2.exe`; `docs/research/banger.md` § "ImpulseLimit2 consumer"):** `ImpulseLimit2` is a *squared contact impulse* — the contact solver tests the squared magnitude of the impulse that would cancel the pair's relative contact-point velocity (`x = K⁻¹·(−v_rel)`, `K` the effective-mass matrix at the contact point) against it, for approaching contacts; crossing it is the one trigger for both outcomes, break (`NumParts > 0`: that many fragment instances, `NumParts` is the spawn bound) and tip/move (`NumParts == 0`: the prop becomes a dynamic pooled body). #1180 read the rest: the gate's first-pass `K` is the first argument's (the striker's) body, and a sub-limit contact is released with no impulse (verified_original; whether another pass responds is not read). Not recovered: the exact weighting. The text below predates this recovery; the striker-kinetic-energy reading it describes is therefore *not* the original quantity. Original open question: what `ImpulseLimit2` is compared against and what crossing it does (break vs. tip vs. nothing) — the implemented striker-kinetic-energy `½·m·v²` estimate is a provisional stand-in (DSN-10), adopted because the placed-prop census shows `ImpulseLimit2 ≈ Mass × {31.25…85342}`, a ladder only a speed-squared quantity reads coherently (F04-C.5, operator report 4 item 3); whether `NumParts` bounds spawned fragments or only echoes the PKG's BREAK count, whether fragments spawn at the activation edge or at a later break threshold (implemented: at activation, one `ImpulseLimit2` gate for both), the `ColliderId`/`AudioId`/`TexNumber` id spaces, `SpinAxis`/`BillFlags`/`Flash`/`YRadius` semantics, the `type: a` tag, when `BirthRule` fires relative to the dormant→active transition, how `default.dgbangerdata` fallback selection works, how a stamped placement acquires `INST_BANGER`, the active-entity lifetime beyond the recovered release tests (`[+0x2b4] == 0` or Y < −100, `docs/research/banger.md`; the 6.0/30000.0 `Timer` despawn is a dead branch — flag never non-zero) and the ×32 active-pool reclaim order, and whether the 4 fragment records carrying `NumParts>0` mean fragments-of-fragments or inert leftovers. The record↔geometry link, placement binding and R4's recovered class structure are verified; the state machine's thresholds are not. |
| UNK-23 | Surface-material runtime semantics (WLD-19): which consumer performs the texture→`materials.csv`→`materials.mtl` lookup for each surface class (PSDL rooms, PKG bound materials, INST/banger colliders), what material an unmapped texture name gets (`_default` vs none — 5 authored PSDL names and `sliver` have no row), what the two dead refs (`ash`, `mud`) resolve to, the `sound` class table behind values 0/1/2, the `width`/`height`/`depth` volume semantics, the `ptxindex`/`ptxthreshold` runtime trigger semantics (the index space itself is recovered — the exe's `ptx_wheel`-adjacent `dirt,dust,grass,leaf,smoke,snow,splash,rock` string block, DSN-62 — native visual-slip comparison and strict `>` are recovered in WLD-36; continuous InitialBlast emission is recovered in WLD-36; native emitter means are recovered in WLD-36; particle variance/integration remain designed), whether `effect` ever differs from `none`, how `friction`/`elasticity`/`drag` combine with tire parameters in the original force path, and whether the `<stem>-NNNN` frame→base lookup matches original name normalization or the original strips suffixes differently. `dirt`/`wood` definitions exist but no csv row references them — their entry point (bound materials? fallback?) is unverified. Runtime note (F06-A): the implemented importer classifies PSDL colliders by authored material index and gives `none`/blank/unmapped/dead-ref slots `SurfaceMaterial::Unspecified` — a documented conservative *implementation* policy, not an answer to the `_default` question above. Runtime note (F06-B): the tire path consumes `friction` normalized against the `_default` block (`_default` → 1.0; retail water ≈ 0.76, deepwater ≈ 0.72), multiplied by a separate environment term (`TireConditions`, `--traction` dev override pending F18) — an *implementation* scaling, not a recovered original rule. Runtime note (F06-B.2): `drag` is consumed raw as a per-wheel viscous wading resistance (`drag × load` opposing contact-plane motion — only water/deepwater carry it on retail, so a car crawls ~1 m/s on the Thames surface while dry land is unchanged) and `elasticity` is consumed as the collider's Avian restitution scaled ×0.1 (the same conservative cap `convert` applies to `BoundElasticity`) — both *implementation* mappings, not recovered original formulas. Runtime note (F07-B.4): `sound` now has a consumer — `SurfaceTables::sound_index` reads it as the positional index into the weather-bound surface table (DSN-39/DSN-43) for the wheel-contact skid/rolling rig, a designed binding under UNK-25. Runtime note (F18-B.4): `ptxindex`/`ptxthreshold` now have a consumer — `SurfaceTables::ptx_channels` resolves the pair per wheel contact into `mm2_game::effects::WheelPtx` channels gated on `tire_slippage` with a strict `>` (DSN-62), a designed reading under this UNK. Runtime note (F06-C): classification is one function for every consumer — `SurfaceTables::classify` maps an `Authored(i)` past the table to `Unspecified`, and `tire_surface_for`, `sound_index`, `ptx_channels` and `restitution_for` all read through it — so a stale or wire-borne index resolves to the same conservative `_default` everywhere (neutral tire grip, the `_default` skid voice, the `_default` particle channels) instead of gripping neutral while sounding and dusting nothing; the voice leg deliberately replaced F06-B's silent reading. A mod layer may also replace `city/materials.{csv,mtl}` on its own: a re-authored `friction` reaches the colliders' `TireSurface` while the `SurfaceMaterial` identity is unchanged, and re-pointing a texture row moves physics without touching a texture (F06-AC03's converse). Both are designed implementation policies under this UNK, not recovered original behaviour. `effect` and `width`/`height`/`depth` still have no consumer. |

| UNK-36 | `camTrackCS` runtime semantics (HUD-3): **partly recovered** from `Midtown2.exe` — full write-up with addresses in `docs/research/camtrack.md`. *verified_original* (read in the code): the offset↔field table; the per-frame order (`Update` `0x51db30`); `MinSpeed`/`MaxSpeed` are m/s (`carsim+0x248`) and interpolate the live follow rate `AppXZPos` between `MaxAppXZPos` (stopped) and `MinAppXZPos` (fast), slewed at `AppInc`/`AppDec` per second — they do **not** extend the boom (DSN-56's speed-window reading is contradicted); the eye follows the desired position per axis at `AppXZPos`/`AppYPos` with the `AppPosMin` knee and `AppApp` low-pass, then `[MinDist, MaxDist]` is a hard post-approach distance clamp that `MinMaxOn` does **not** gate; `MinMaxOn` is a separate ±5 m vertical ground/ceiling clamp of the eye; `CollideType` has code for exactly 1 (instant five-ray pull-in along the aim→eye line, 0.33 m margin) and 2 (eased single-ray pull-in), never clip/reposition, stock records are all 1; `TrackTo` is car-local through the full car matrix and `Offset` is a **yaw-only, gravity-aligned** offset from the aim point (`+X` right, `+Y` up, `+Z` behind), so car pitch/roll never tilt the boom; `LookAbove` is overwritten each frame with `(Offset.y−0.8)·VertOffset`; `HillMin/HillMax/HillLerp` shape a ground-slope pitch bias; the reverse swing orbits to ±π at `RevOnApp`/back at `RevOffApp` rad/s after a hard-coded 2.0 s in reverse gear (`RevDelay` is never read); `TrackBreak` 1/2 break tracking (1: body up-axis y < 0.35); `MinHardSteer`, `DriftDelay`, `FrontRate`, `RearRate`, `FlipDelay`, `SteerOn`/`SteerMin`/`SteerAmt` and `RevDelay` have no reader in the camera code (`0x51d000..0x525000`). *inferred*: the orientation approach (`AppRot`/`AppXRot`/`AppRotMin`/`LookAt` blend), the hit-result field layout behind the vertical clamp. *unknown*: the airborne/re-anchor flags' exact triggers (`0x190`/`0x194`/`0x198`, speed² `2.25e6` test), the `0x522b60` clamp veto, near↔far lens selection and `BlendTime`/`BlendGoal`. **Bound (F22, `mm2_app::camera::chase_follow`):** the speed→follow-rate lerp and `AppInc`/`AppDec` slew (`follow_rate_target`/`slew_follow_rate`), the yaw-only `Offset` frame around the car-local `TrackTo` (`desired_eye`), the per-axis approach with `AppPosMin` knee and `AppApp` low-pass (`approach_axis`, `AppXZPos` for X/Z, `AppYPos` for Y; the low-pass is applied as `1−(1−AppApp)^(60·dt)` so it is frame-rate independent and identical at the original's fixed 1/60 s), the ungated `[MinDist, MaxDist]` eye–aim clamp and the `LookAbove` look target; omitted record fields take the constructor defaults. Also bound (#1212): the reverse swing (`camera::ReverseSwing`: 2.0 s constant delay with throttle in reverse gear, side from steering > 0.1, ramp to `±π` at `RevOnApp` and back at `RevOffApp`, `ReverseOn` −1 pin, handbrake > 0.5 in reverse zeroes `AppXZPos`; the orbit direction is recovered — `0x4bce50` is `this = this·R(axis, angle)` with the axis normalised inside the builder `0x4bcf90` (`0x4bd085` divides by `|axis|`), a right-handed rotation `Quat::from_axis_angle` reproduces) and the `TrackBreak` verdict (recorded, not consumed — its readers are the unrecovered flag machine). Also bound (#1297): the hill bias (`camera::hill_mid`/`hill_normal`/`hill_bias`/`hill_pitch` — `0x51dec0` averages the grounded front/rear axle **hit normals** (`carsim` wheel `+0xcc`, front slots `0`/`1`, rear `2`/`3`, each group counting only while its mean clears `len² > 0.01`, `0x5af358`; both groups → their mean, one → that group, neither → `(0,1,0)`), low-passes them at `HillLerp` (`0x170`, snapping while the reset flag `0xc8` is set) and normalises in place; then `θ = acos(clamp(up·n))` (`0x4c0420`), `c = up × n`, `v = min(1, 4θ/π)`, `s = v·(carX·c)` (`carX` = the car matrix's row `0`), a `sin`/`cos` ease gives `u` (`1 − cos(min(1,2s)·π/2)/2` for `s > 0`, `sin((1−min(1,2|s|))·π/2)/2` for `s < 0`, `0.5` at `s = 0`) and the `0x1b4` bias is `−HillMin·(1−2u)` for `u < 0.5` else `−HillMax·(2u−1)`; `0x51e3e0` rotates the eye about `up × boom` by `−P`, `P = bias·(1−2f)` with `f = clamp(|swing|/π)`, pivoting on the aim and *before* the world-up swing, so it *flips* sign at a full reverse swing; the port's snap uses its teleport/first-frame `jumped` rather than the retail `0xc8`). Still our own: the orientation approach (`AppRot`/`AppXRot` easing — the look is applied directly), the airborne states, and the `0.4 m` look raise whose relation to `LookAbove` is unresolved. Also bound (#1211): the `MinMaxOn` ±5 m vertical clamp (`min_max_clamp`: ceiling `hit.y−0.5`, floor `hit.y+0.5`, floor wins) and `CollideType` 1 (instant five-ray pull-in to `hit + near − 0.33` along the aim→eye line) / 2 (single ray, squared distance eased at ±30·dt) / other = nothing (`collide_eye`); the own-trailer exclusion is kept. *Inferred* in that binding: the four extra rays go to the corners of the near-plane rectangle around the eye (the original scales the camera right/up axes by frustum globals not yet resolved), the ray length is `MaxDist` (the eye distance when absent), the `CollideType 2` ray/goal reuse type 1's `hit + near − 0.33`, and avian's `normal.y` stands in for the hit block's `+0x10` component. The implemented boom/speed-window/occlusion mapping is a designed reading (DSN-56), including the occlusion ray's exclusion of the player's *own* trailer entities (part of the rig — a trailered car's authored near boom lands inside the trailer box; vpsemi `_near` `Offset` z=7.73 vs a ~14 m trailer — so counting it parked the camera in the hitch gap or behind the trailer's rear wall; measured broken on retail captures, fixed F22-B.4). Other vehicles' trailers still occlude like any world object. |

| UNK-43 | Pedestrian car-sensing and avoidance rule (F19-B.3, F19-B.7, #1285, 2026-10-14): **recovered** from `Midtown2.exe` (disassembly; addresses and reading in `docs/research/pedanim.md` "Car sensing"). *verified_original* (per-frame ped update `0x54b9a0`): a walker walks the linked list at `0x6b1f60` (manager `0x6b1df0` +0x170, nodes threaded at +0x2c) and keeps the nearest by horizontal squared distance; a node carries its car actor at +0x14 and its player index word at +0x8, the index into the four-slot **player** table at `0x6b1df0` +0xb0 (accessor `0x534ad0`, which asserts `Returning a NULL Player. Idx: %d` at `0x5d652c`), so only player-driven cars are sensed — AI opponents use managers `0x534920`/`0x534970` and ambient traffic holds no player slot. It ignores cars at ≥ 35 m (1225 m²) and slower than 1 m/s. The near corridor (`0x54e660`: along-axis `L` in (0.25 × length `+0x224`, 20), lateral inside 0.5 × width `+0x21c` + 2) gives *dive* (state 2) when the time value < 0.75, else *wary* (state 1) below 2.3; the far corridor (`0x54e8a0`: `L` in (0.25 × length, 35), lateral inside 0.5 × width + 4) gives wary only. The time value (`0x54e630`) is the per-car factor at +0x24 × (`L` − 2), and that factor is `1.0 / speed` (`0x53f400`, `1.0 / sim+0x248` once the speed exceeds 0.001), so the value is seconds to contact. The dive (`0x54d8b0`) faces the walker along the car's axis and picks the side at `0x54dc3f`: the car's steering field +0x1554 (`+1` right) > 0.85 (`0x5b1e34`) ⇒ `*_RDIVE`, < −0.85 (`0x5b4880`) ⇒ `*_LDIVE` (both strict), else the stored side value at walker +0x30 > 0 ⇒ `*_LDIVE`, ≤ 0 ⇒ `*_RDIVE`; `WALK_*DIVE` when the walker's clip is `WALK`, else `ANTIC_*DIVE` (state strings resolved at `0x54b200..`). Wary faces the car. *inferred*: which side `R` means in the walker's own frame (the port renders the non-steering fallback as the walker's own side of the car's line). *unknown*: the corridor fields' own names, the +0x304 direction flag's second sign convention, the wary/dive exit timers, root-motion vs bookkeeping for the dive, whether a diver can land in traffic. Bound in `mm2_game::pedreact`: sense range 35 m, min speed 1 m/s, alert 2.3 s, dive 0.75 s, both corridor shapes (`NEAR_RANGE`/`FAR_RANGE`/`NEAR_MARGIN`/`FAR_MARGIN`/`NOSE_FRACTION`/`TIME_OFFSET`), the steering side (`DIVE_STEER`) and player-only filtering; the exit timers stay designed (DSN-89). and the nearest-car selection (`nearest_car`/`sensed_threat`, #1428): only the nearest player car by horizontal squared distance is assessed, so a nearer parked or off-line car hides a farther striking one. | verified_original | `Midtown2.exe` 0x54b9a0/0x54ba4a/0x54bc1a/0x54e660/0x54e8a0/0x54e630/0x53f400/0x54dc3f; `mm2_game::pedreact`; docs/research/pedanim.md "Car sensing" |
| UNK-44 | Cable cars (F28-A): `Midtown2.exe` creates cable cars in the AI map init (`"AIMAP.Init: Create the cable cars."` at `0x5d66d0`, pushed at `0x5358cf`; model `va_cablecar_f` at `0x5d66f4`) and ships `cablecar`/`cablecarstart`/`cablecarstop`/`cablecarbell*`/`streetcable` audio; no per-city pathset, not in either city's ambient roster. **Recovered (verified_original):** one car per BAI intersection with exactly one tram-carrying road (`0x54a200`; ctor `0x53f7c0`) — 4 in San Francisco, 0 in London; the next-road rule at a road end (`0x53fcb0`: 1 tram road → turn round, 2 → the other, 4 → straight across, else none — SF has only 1/2/4, giving two closed circuits of 26 and 16 legs); the speed controller (`0x540560`: cruise 15 m/s, look-ahead 25 m, own acceleration 1.5 + 2·rand/32768 m/s², brake to stop 0.25 m short of a closed road end or 2.5 m behind an obstacle — any other AI vehicle in a 30 m × 2 m probe ahead —, no braking between roads). Implemented as a partial runtime (`mm2_app::cablecar`, `mm2-inspect specials`; the signal timing it waits on is now recovered — F10-SIGNAL-TIMING, UNK-12). **Height (measured, report 7 item 3):** the tram curve is the road-surface datum — a raycast down from 3 m above each 10 m of both SF circuits' curves hits the carriageway at the curve's own height (median gap 0.0 m; the ±1–2.8 m tails are junction/bridge/kerb samples) — and `va_cablecar_f` is authored with its origin at the floor (PKG `BODY_H` y 0.0014..3.29, `.bnd` y −0.002..3.32), so the body rides at the curve. ## Gaps in this ledger

- `MM2HELP.HLP` topics on joystick/gamepad/wheel button maps and
  serial/modem setup are documented but not yet transcribed into rules;
  they matter for F23, not yet needed.
- No observable-behavior verification yet (no original executable run,
  no packet capture): everything marked *documented* is doc-trust only.
- Race *names* shown in the UI are unverified (UNK-7) — the CSV
  `Description` column is `none` for all race events.
